# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_18651589.pdf
1
RESOLUTION NO. ________
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION APPROVING A FIRST AMENDMENT TO INDENTURE
OF TRUST AND CERTAIN OTHER DOCUMENTS AND MATTERS
RELATED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, REFUNDING
REVENUE BONDS (ST. MARY’S SCHOOL PROJECT) SERIES 2015.”
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (the “City”) is a municipality duly organized
and existing under the Constitution and laws of the State of Wyoming (the “State”); and
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyoming Statute §§ 15-1-701 through 15-1-710 (the “Act”) and
Wyoming Statute §§ 16-5-501, et seq., the City previously issued its $9,000,000 City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming Variable Rate Revenue Bonds (St. Mary’s School Project), Series 2007 (the “Series 2007
Bonds”) and loaned the proceeds of the Series 2007 Bonds to the Church of St. Mary, a Wyoming
non-profit corporation (the “Borrower”) to finance the construction, equipping and furnishing of an
elementary school in Cheyenne, Wyoming; and
WHEREAS, pursuant to the Act, the City previously issued its $7,915,000 City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, Refunding Revenue Bonds (St. Mary’s School Project), Series 2015 (the “Series 2015
Bond”), pursuant to an Ordinance No. 4079 adopted by the Governing Body of the City (the
“Governing Body”) on June 22, 2015 (the “Ordinance”), and an Indenture of Trust, dated as of June
1, 2015, by and between the City and Wells Fargo Bank, National Association, as trustee (the
“Trustee”), and loaned the proceeds of the Series 2015 Bond to the Borrower to finance the
redeeming and refunding of the outstanding principal balance of the Series 2007 Bonds, together
with any unpaid interest thereon; and
WHEREAS, the Series 2015 Bond was issued, and is currently held in full by, Wells Fargo
Bank, National Association, as bondholder (the “Purchaser”); and 
WHEREAS, a change to the Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate has impacted the
Medium Term Rate currently charged on the Series 2015 Bond (the “Rate Increase”); and
WHEREAS, in response to such Rate Increase, the Purchaser has agreed to a reduction in
the base Medium Term Rate currently charged on the Series 2015 Bond, together with certain
additional modifications to the interest rate provisions of the Indenture; and
WHEREAS, Section 8.02 of the Indenture permits the Indenture to be amended to reduce
the interest rate on the Series 2015 Bond with the consent of the Owners of the outstanding Series
2015 Bond; and
WHEREAS, the Borrower has requested the City facilitate the execution and delivery of a
First Amendment to Indenture of Trust (the “First Amendment”) dated as of the date thereof by and
between the City and the Trustee, for the purposes of modifying the Medium Term Rate; and 
WHEREAS, the Purchaser, as the Owner of 100% of the Outstanding Bond, will consent to
this First Amendment; and
WHEREAS, the execution of the First Amendment will result in a reissuance of the Series
2015 Bond for federal income tax purposes and, as a result thereof, in conjunction herewith, (a) the
City and the Borrower will enter into a Tax Regulatory Agreement and No-Arbitrage Certificate (the
“Tax Certificate”), (b) the City and Hathaway & Kunz, P.C., and Dinsmore & Shohl, LLP, together,
serving as co-Bond Counsel (referred to herein collectively as “Bond Counsel”), will prepare and
submit an IRS Form 8038, (c) Bond Counsel will give its bond opinion, and (d) the Borrower will
cause to be delivered to the City and Bond Counsel a borrower’s counsel opinion.
WHEREAS, the Series 2015 Bond is not, does not and will not constitute or become an
indebtedness, a debt or a pecuniary liability of or a charge against the general credit or taxing power
of the City or the State within the meaning of any provision or limitation of the State constitution
or statutes; and

2
WHEREAS, the City has been presented with the proposed form of the First Amendment
to be used in connection with the reduction of the Medium Term Rate; and
WHEREAS, capitalized terms used but not defined herein have the respective meanings
ascribed to them in the Indenture; and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body desires to authorize the execution by the City of the First
Amendment in substantially the form presented to the City and further desires to authorize to
approve the participation by the City in such other transactions as are contemplated thereby, all in
accordance with the provisions of the Indenture, as amended and the Act.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT:
Section 1.  Approval of First Amendment; Authorization for Execution.  The form, terms and
provisions of the First Amendment are hereby approved and the Mayor and the officials of the City,
including the City Attorney, or their respective written designee (each an “Authorized Officer”) shall
enter into the First Amendment in substantially the form of the document presented to the Governing
Body, with only such changes therein as are not inconsistent herewith; provided, however, that the
Authorized Officers are empowered to make or accept any changes to any such First Amendment
that such Authorized Officer deems necessary, such determination to be evidenced by the execution
thereof by such Authorized Officer of such document as so modified; and, provided further, that the
Authorized Officer is hereby authorized to execute and deliver the First Amendment as required,
and the Clerk of the City is hereby authorized to attest on behalf of the City the First Amendment.
Section 2.  Other Actions by the City.  Each Authorized Officer and all officers, agents and
employees of the City, for and on behalf of the City, be and they hereby are authorized and directed
to do any and all things necessary to effect the execution and delivery of each of the First
Amendment and to carry out the terms thereof, in conformity with the statutes of the State, necessary
or reasonably required by the parties to the First Amendment to effectuate its provisions and shall
take all action necessary or desirable to facilitate the rate modification and for carrying out the
transactions contemplated by this First Amendments.  Each Authorized Officer and all officers,
agents and employees of the City are further authorized and directed, for and on behalf of the City,
to execute all papers, documents, certificates and other instruments that may be required in order
to carry out the authority conferred by this Resolution and the First Amendment including, without
limitation, the execution and delivery of any required certificates, tax agreements, including the Tax
Certificate, financing statements, or other closing documents to be delivered in connection with the
execution and delivery of the Amendments.
Section 3.  No Indebtedness of the City.  The Series 2015 Bond shall not constitute or
become an indebtedness, a debt or a pecuniary liability of or a charge against the general credit or
taxing power of the City or the State within the meaning of any provision or limitation of the State
constitution or statutes.  Nothing contained in this Resolution, the Ordinance, the Series 2015 Bond,
the Indenture, as amended, shall give rise to any personal or pecuniary liability of any officer,
employee or agent of the City.
Section 4.  Severability.  The provisions of this Resolution are hereby declared to be
severable and if any section, phrase or provisions shall for any reason be declared to be invalid, such
declaration shall not affect the validity of the remainder of the sections, phrases and provisions
hereof.
Section 5.  Governing Law.  This Resolution shall be construed and governed in accordance
with the laws of the State.
Section 6.  Repeal of Inconsistent Resolutions.  All other resolutions of the Governing Body,
or parts of resolutions, inconsistent with this Resolution, are hereby repealed to the extent of such
inconsistency.
Section 7.  Effective Date of Resolution.  This Resolution shall take effect from and after its
passage and approval.

3
Section 8.  Effective Date.  This resolution shall be in full force and effect upon its approval
and publication.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of _________________, 2018.
__________________________________________
Marian J. Orr, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
____________________________________
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

FIRST AMENDMENT TO INDENTURE OF TRUST
This FIRST AMENDMENT TO INDENTURE OF TRUST (this “First Amendment’), dated as of
June
, 2018 (the “First Amendment Effective Date”), is by and between the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming (the “Issuer”), and Wells Fargo Bank, National Association, duly organized and existing under
the laws of the United States of America, as trustee (the “Trustee”), and amends that certain Indenture of
Trust, dated as of June 1, 2015, by and between the Issuer and the Trustee (the “Indenture”).
Capitalized terms used but not defined herein have the respective meanings ascribed to them in the
Indenture.
WITNESSETH:
WHEREAS, pursuant to the Indenture, the Issuer issued its $7,915,000 City of Cheyenne (Saint Mary’s
School Project), Series 2015 (the “Bond”), and loaned the proceeds of the Bond to the Church of St.
Mary, a Wyoming nonprofit corporation (the “Borrower”), pursuant to that certain Loan Agreement,
dated as of June 1, 2015 (the “Loan Agreement”); and
WHEREAS, the Bond was issued to, and is currently held in hill by, Wells Fargo Bank, National
Association, as bondholder (the “Purchaset”); and
WHEREAS, a change to the Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate has impacted the Medium Term Rate
currently charged on the Bond (the “Rate Increase”); and
WHEREAS, in response to such Rate Increase, the Purchaser has agreed to a reduction in the base
Medium Term Rate currently charged on the Bond, together with certain additional modifications to the
interest rate provisions of the Indenture; and
WHEREAS, Section 8.02 of the Indenture permits the Indenture to be amended to reduce the interest rate
on the Bond with the consent of the Owners of all of the Outstanding Bond; and
WHEREAS, the Purchaser, as the Owner of 100% of the Outstanding Bond, has consented to this First
Amendment, as evidenced by the consent attached hereto as Exhibit A; and
WHEREAS, the amendments set forth herein will result in a reissuance of the Bond for federal income
tax purposes (the “Tar Reissuance “) and, as a result thereof, in conjunction herewith, (a) the Issuer and
the Borrower will enter into a Tax Regulatory Agreement and No-Arbitrage Certificate, (b) the Issuer and
Hathaway & Kunz, P.C. and Dinsmore & Shohl, LLP, together, serving as co-Bond Counsel (referred to
herein collectively as “Bond Counsel”), will prepare and submit an IRS Form 8038, (c) Bond Counsel
will give its bond opinion, and (d) the Borrower will cause to be delivered to the Issuer and Bond Counsel
a borrower’s counsel opinion.

NOW, TREREFORE, TifiS FIRST AMENDMENT WITNESSETH:
ARTICLE I.
AMENDMENTS
SECTION 1.1. Amendment to Definitions. The definitions of “Medium Term Rare “, “Taxable
Rare “, and “Margin Rare Facror” in Section 1.01 of the Indenture are deleted in their entirety and
replaced with the following as of the First Amendment Effective Date:
“Margin Rare Facror” means the greater of (a) 1.0, and (b) the product of (i) one minus
the prevailing Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate in effect on the date of calculation
multiplied by (ii) the quotient of(A) one divided by (B) one minus the Maximum Federal
Corporate Tax Rate on the First Amendment Effective Date. The effective date of any
change in the Margin Rate Factor shall be the effective date of the decrease or increase
(as applicable) in the Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate resulting in such change.
The Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate as of April 27, 2018 is 21% such that the
current Margin Rate Factor equals 1.0. The Margin Rate Factor will not go below 1.0.
“Medium Term Rare” means (a) during the initial Direct Purchase Period occurring on or
subsequent to June —, 2018, [3.35%] multiplied by the Margin Rate Factor, (b) during
any other Direct Purchase Period, the interest rate on the Bond established from time to
time pursuant to Section 2.03(d) multiplied by the Margin Rate Factor and (c) during any
Rate Period that is not a Direct Purchase Period, the interest rate on the Bond established
from time to time pursuant to Section 2.03(d).
“Taxable Rare” means for any date of determination, the rate of interest per annum equal
to the product of the interest rate on the Bonds then in effect multiplied by the quotient of
(a) one divided by (b) one minus the prevailing Maximum Federal Corporate Tax Rate.
ARTICLE H.
MISCELLANEOUS
SECTEON 2.1. Bond Document. This First Amendment is a Bond Document.
SECTION 2.2. Governing Law. This First Amendment shall be governed by and interpreted in
accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming.
SECTION 23. Effect on Indenture. The Indenture, as amended hereby, remains in full force
and effect in accordance with its terms and is ratified and confirmed. The execution and delivery of this
First Amendment does not and will not operate as a modification or waiver of any right, power, or remedy
of Trustee or the Issuer under the Indenture or any other Bond Document, except as specifically set forth
herein.
Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, except as provided in this First Amendment,
nothing herein limits or affects the obligations and limitations contained in the Indenture.
The
modifications herein are limited to the specifics hereof.
SECTION 2.4. Execution in Counterparts. This First Amendment may be executed in several
counterparts, each of which shall be an original and all of which shall constitute but one and the same
instrument.
Delivery of an executed counterpart of this First Amendment by electronic mail will be
equally effective as delivery of an original executed counterpart of this First Amendment. A party
delivering an executed counterpart of this First Amendment by electronic mail shall also deliver an
2

original executed counterpart of this First Amendment, but the failure to deliver an original executed
counterpart will not affect the validity, enforceability, and binding effect of this First Amendment.

THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this
day of________________ 2018, by
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001
(“City”), and Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects, located at 2515 Warren Avenue, Suite 504,
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“Consultant”).
RECITALS
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and
between the parties as follows:
1.
SCOPE OF WORK.
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference
and expressly made a part hereof
2.
TIME OF PERFORMANCE.
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by February 28, 2021. The
services will be completed within the stipulated period indicated in the attached Exhibit C,
Project Schedule, incorporated by reference and expressly made a part hereof
3.
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY.
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the same
or similar locality. Upon notice to the Consultant and by mutual agreement between the
parties, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not
meeting such a standard.
4.
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS.
The Pasties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.
5.
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE.
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act ofNovember 1988, the City of Cheyenne
has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and drug
usage by City employees. Ml architects, engineers and other consultants under contract with
City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to comply with the
1

City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to comply with the
provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol
usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties
and responsibilities ofthe Agreement. It is the responsibility ofthe Consultant to familiarize
themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-
consultants of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their
employees or sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be
terminated.
6.
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS.
The Consultant agrees to indemnil5’, hold harmless and defend the City from and against any
and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses incident
thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result of death
or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or
adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or
orders to the extent caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this
Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongffil act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its
employees or sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.
The Consultant
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms ofthis
paragraph.
7.
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS.
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages:
Commercial General Liability Insurance
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement.
Business Automobile Insurance
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily
injury and property damage of$ 1,000,000 per accident for the entire term ofthe Agreement.
Workers’ Compensation
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services
under this Agreement.
Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage.
Professional Liability Insurance
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties
under this Agreement. The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.
2

Ifthis coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy:
a.
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution ofthis Agreement, provide the City
with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in
effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this
Agreement; and
b.
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a flinire policy period, the
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this
Agreement; and
c.
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement;
and
d.
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this
paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of
the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for
an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has
provided services under this Agreement.
Additional Insurance Information
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by
endorsement on its insurance policies, and shall provide the City with a copy of the
endorsements. This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional
liability insurance policies.
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement.
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately noti& in writing the city
risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has
resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach
of this Agreement.
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of
insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional
insured. The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates
shall not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder.
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent).
3

8.
MINORiTY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES. 49 C.F.R. Part 26
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in,
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award and
performance ofthis Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national origin
or sex.
9.
COMPENSATION.
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Six
Hundred Fifty Seven Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($657,500.00). The Compensation
Schedule is attached as Exhibit B, incorporated by reference and made a part hereof.
10.
MONTHLY REPORT.
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written
statement ofthe status ofthe work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and work
schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any
payment request until the report is submitted.
11.
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT.
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms ofthis Agreement. Nothing in this
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of any
kind on the behalf of the City.
12.
TAXES.
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement.
13.
DEFAULT.
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default.
14.
REMEDIES.
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default. In the event the default remains
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to:
a.
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not
exceed the contract amount; or
b.
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance.
4

15.
TELM AND TERMINATION.
This Agreement shall be for a period from June 1, 2018 to February 28, 2021. The parties
may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement.
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty (30) days’ written notice to the Consultant,
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall:
a.
Discontinue all services affected; and
b.
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications,
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this Agreement,
whether completed or in process.
c.
In the event oftermination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant
for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of tennination.
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld.
16.
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES.
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event ofdefault
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its own
costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.
17.
GOVERNING LAW. JURISDICTION AND VENUE.
The construction, interpretation and enforcement ofthis Agreement shall be governed by the
laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction
over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the venue shall be the
First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming.
18.
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY.
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarii’ or enforce the written terms of the
Agreement. Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses available
to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-lOt, et seq., and
all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental
immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly
construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental
or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity.
19.
MONITORING ACTIVITIES.
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited to,
the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts and
5

consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to examine
the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; and to
observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work.
20.
NON-DISCRIMINATION.
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With Disabilities
Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, e seq., and the Age Discrimination Act of 1975
and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, and shall not discriminate
against any individual on the grounds ofage, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national origin,

Agreement.
21.
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION.
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary.
22.
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT.
This Agreement consisting of six (6) pages, Exhibit A consisting of five (5) pages, Exhibit
B consisting of one (1) page, Exhibit C consisting of one (1) page, and Exhibit D, contain

EXHIBIT A
SCOPE OF WORK
PROJECT DESCRIPTION.
The responsive proposal from Noel Griffith, Jr. & Associates Architects is attached as Exhibit U,
incorporated by reference and expressly made a part hereof. To the extent that this Agreement and
RFP-3-l 8 are in conflict, this Agreement shall control.
A.
Introduction and Background.
On May 2,2017, the City ofCheyenne approved a 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax
(6th penny) for the design and construction of a new City of Cheyenne Municipal Court.
Consultant will;
I.
Present building design options for the City’s consideration for construction
of a new Municipal Court at 308 West
7’ Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
2.
Assist City staff in preparing the RFP for Construction Manager at Risk
(CMAR) proposals, and assist in the CMAR selection.
3.
Work with the CMAR during project design as the CMAR develops project
estimates and final OMP to remain in project budget.
4.
Meet with City staff and the CMAR to review the GMP proposal.
5.
Meet with City staff and the CMAR to review qualifications of prospective
sub-contract bidders, and to review sub-contract bids.
6.
Participate, in conjunction with CMAR, in preparation of cost estimates in
all phases of design.
B.
Project Objectives.
The objectives of this project are:
1.
Present design options to the City for construction of a new Municipal Court.
2.
Prepare a lull design of the selected site for the new Municipal Court.
3.
Assist the City with the selection and hiring of a CMAR.
1

4.
Prepare construction bidding documents as required by City of Cheyenne’s
Purchasing Office if not included in the CMAR.
5.
Perform construction administration of the building at an hourly rate.
(Mileage and drive time to and from location may not be added to the cost
of construction administration hourly rate.)
C.
Scope of Services.
The minimum scope of services related to this project include the following:
1.
The Consultant shall meet with City staff to gain an understanding of the
desired work.
2.
The Consultant shall Issue an RFP for a CMAR interviewing and selecting
a CMAR.
3.
The Consultant shall be responsible for completing a fill design for the new
Municipal Court.
4.
The Consultant shall prepare an operations manual to ensure optimal
operations of the facility.
Suggested comments and additions should be
provided by the Consultant to enhance the City’s ability to operate the
facility in an efficient and safe manner.
5.
The Consultant shall meet frequently (at least on a bi-monthly basis) with
City staff during the design phase and on a weekly basis during the
Construction Administration phase.
13.
Sub-Consultants.
The Consultant shall be responsible to retain and pay for the services of any sub-consultant
necessary to complete the work. The City and the Consultant shall mutually agree to the use
of any sub-consultants which the Consultant desires to retain.
E.
Meetings.
The Consultant shall meet with members of the City staff at least on a hi-monthly basis
during design phase and on a weekly basis during the construction administration phase to
keep all current on the progress of the project. The Consultant may also be required to meet
with the City Council to discuss the project or recommendations.
2

II.
PROJECT BUDGET.
The total budget for the project includes design, City of Cheyenne bidding process, new Municipal
Court design and construction, and Construction Administration on a hourly basis. Total ifinding
from the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax is $9,000,000.00. The total cost of the Municipal
Court shall not exceed the $9,000,000.00 unless expressly agreed to in writing by the City of
Cheyenne Governing Body.
III.
GENERAL CONDITIONS.
The following items are considered to be included as part ofthe basic compensation for this project:
A.
Normal business expenses, i.e., phone, postage, etc.
B.
Cost of insurance.
C.
In-house computer time and service.
D.
Word processing, accounting, and man-hour records.
E.
Permits and license fees.
F.
Mileage.
G.
Travel fees, room and board, per diem.
H.
Printing costs for all standard review, bidding, and as-constructed plans and other
correspondence and contract documents.
I.
Film and processing.
J.
Overtime engineering and inspection.
K.
Special inspections, including vacuum excavation pot-holing.
L.
Survey materials, stakes, etc.
M.
Additional required services.
Any changes in the Consultants or sub-consultants staffor fee structure shall be presented in writing
to the City for approval prior to initialing any changes or performing any work.
3

IV.
PRELIMINARY DESIGN PHASE.
Upon receipt ofNotice to Proceed with this project, the Consultant shall proceed with the following
tasks:
A.
Furnish five (5) copies ofthe preliminary design for review by the City ofCheyenne.
B.
Obtain a CMAR.
C.
Advise City of estimated Project Cost. The Consultant shall provide a summary of
the probable Project Cost based on the Preliminary Design Drawings and
Specifications showing estimated construction quantities, estimated bid prices, and
items of work. The bid tabulation shall be laid out using standard numbering for
work items and basic pay units.
The services will be completed and the data submitted within the stipulated period indicated in
Exhibit C, Project Schedule.
V.
FINAL DESIGN PHASE.
The City of Cheyenne will review the preliminary design and cost estimate. The City ofCheyenne
will then provide Consultant with comments, suggestions and/or modifications. The Consultant
will, upon receipt ofwritten authorization from the City ofCheyenne, proceed with final design and
shall:
A.
Prepare final drawings showing the character and extent of the project and
specifications incorporating any modifications or changes indicated during review
of preliminary documents.
B.
Advise the City of Cheyenne of any adjustments to the latest opinion of estimated
project cost caused by changes in extent or design requirements of the project. The
Consultant shall provide a summary of the probable project cost based on the Final
Design Drawings and Specifications showing estimated construction quantities, bid
prices, and items of work.
The bid tabulation shall be laid out using standard
numbering for work items and basic pay units.
C.
Furnish five (5) copies of the above final design documents then present and review
them in person with the City of Cheyenne.
Vt
CONSTRUCTION ADMINISTRATION PHASE.
A.
Prepare City of Cheyenne Bidding and Construction documents with a CMAR.
B.
Conduct a Pre-Bid meeting to answer questions associated with the project.
4

C.
Review all submitted bids and provide the City of Cheyenne with a detailed analysis.
Include a recommendation of responsible successffil bidder.
D.
Attend City Council and Finance Committee meetings (as necessary) to provide
detailed information on successffil bidder’s documentation.
E.
Administer, conduct and inspect all work under the City’s Bidding and Construction
documents. Cost of onsite inspection to be shown as a separate itemized hourly
price.
F.
Conduct training for City personnel on operations and maintenance at the new
Municipal Court.
The services will be completed and the data submitted within the stipulated period indicated in
Exhibit C, Project Schedule.
5

Attention: Craig LaVoy, Deputy Public Works Director  
Re: RFP-3-18 new Municipal Court Addition and/or Renovation – Technical Proposal 
 
Dear Mr. LaVoy and Members of the Selection Committee, 
 
Noel Griffith, Jr. & Associates Architects (NGA), in association with Reilly Johnson Architecture (RJA) is 
pleased to submit our response to your Request for Proposals to provide the professional services for the 
programming of space requirements, and design, for the New Municipal Court Addition and/or 
Renovation.  And, as requested in the City’s RFP, our Price Proposal is being submitted separately. 
To achieve your project goals, we have brought together a highly experienced and enthusiastic team.  
They have project specific expertise and will offer you the highest level of service.  Our Design Team has 
the resources required to provide the appropriate services needed for a successful project.  We have the 
key personnel available and capable to dedicate to your project, and these same professionals will be 
involved throughout your project to see you through to completion. 
We are pleased to have Reilly Johnson Architecture on our Design Team as the Associate Architect to lead 
the Design Team in the planning and design of the Municipal Court, and to assist through construction of 
the Project.  RJA has specialized in the planning, programming, and design of Justice Facilities for almost 
30 years.  This is not one of many specializations within their firm - it is their only specialization, and we 
wanted our Team to bring such special expertise to this Project.  Therefore, because we know Reilly 
Johnson Architecture well, since our first time working together in 2000|2001 on the $9M Laramie County 
Law Enforcement + Detention Center Expansion + Remodel, and since they have such expertise, we invited 
them to join our Design Team.  A select list of their courts projects is presented in Section E of this 
Proposal, and all the projects listed were planned and designed by two of their principals - Richard Backes 
and Robert Johnson - both of whom will play key roles in this Project. 
Among others, our Design Team also includes Cator|Ruma & Associates of Cheyenne and Denver as 
mechanical and electrical engineers; and KL&A of Buffalo, WY and Denver as structural engineers.  NGA 
and RJA have worked on many projects with these two consultants. 
With that introduction, we would like to make a few comments about your Project.  First of all, we are 
keenly aware of the February 1st deadline to have the Municipal Court relocated to 2101 O’Neil Avenue 
and for many reasons we are confident of our Design Team’s ability to make that date.  One thing we 
would like to offer to that end, is our Team’s readiness to begin work with the City in advance of a fully-
executed Design Services Agreement.  This means we are prepared, and enthused, about starting on 
September 15 instead of waiting for the City’s formal Notice To Proceed (NTP) of October 11.  This “early 
start” will allow us to assist the City with the RFP for the CMAR services, and to tentatively hold the Scoping 
Meeting on Monday, September 18th.  If the City will allow this “early start”, we gain almost a month, 
which will be of great help in ensuring the Court is relocated by February 1. 

 
 
 
 
 
2515 warren ave.     •     suite 504     •     cheyenne    wyoming     •     82001     •     (307) 632-2705 
fax # (307) 632-4375     •     e-mail: ngriffith@noelgriffith.com   •    www.noelgriffith.com  
NGA and RJA have spent considerable time reviewing the Court’s current space, and in examining 2101 
O’Neil, and we believe we already have some concepts for the Courts relocation that with a little further 
study, and input, may meet with the City’s approval.  With this “early start” we expect to be able to present 
these concepts to the City on, about, September 25th.  We would also like to concurrently start the Concept 
Design the Addition and/or Renovation as well. 
Although we understand there is more ground to cover, we do have a solid understanding of the project 
thus far, based on the requirements laid out in the RFP.  We are ready to hit the ground running, and start 
further evaluating functional, operational and design options for consideration.  We are committed to this 
project and will dedicate our full resources to ensure the success of this project.   
We very enthusiastically hope for the opportunity to work with the City on this very interesting and 
challenging Project. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
Noel Griffith, Jr. AIA 
Architect/Principal

The firm of Noel Griffith Jr & Associates Architects (NGA) 
was founded in Cheyenne, Wyoming in 1976 and has 
grown steadily through the years.  NGA is a well-
rounded, full-service architectural design firm providing 
not only architectural design services, but also interior 
design services, master planning, feasibility studies, cost 
estimating, and construction administration. 
Throughout the years NGA has gained a solid reputation 
for excellence in providing professional services through-
out the entire project from schematic design and space 
planning to construction management and project com-
pletion.  We have experience in several different con-
struction management methods including; Construction 
Manager at Risk (CMAR), Design-Build (DB), Design-Bid-
Build, Construction Manager (CM), and negotiated con-
tracts.   
NGA has a tremendous amount of experience working 
with various public and government entities, and is well 
versed with the standard procedures and procurements.  
We also have a proven ability to prepare accurate and 
realistic cost estimates, which is vital to ensure that the 
appropriated funding is properly applied to this Project 
and that this Project completes under the established 
construction budget.   
NGA is small enough to provide prompt, personal atten-
tion to each project through hands-on principal involve-
ment, yet large enough to facilitate effective delivery of 
projects in an array of scope and size.  We maintain a 
staff of five; two architects, two project managers, and 
an office manager.  Organization is utilized to stress ex-
cellence in design and is balanced with rigorous project 
management to provide each client the personal atten-
tion required to achieve project success.   
NGA utilizes state-of-the-art technology to enhance the 
design process.  This combined with the in-depth 
knowledge achieved from our vast experience will pro-
vide you with a Design Team who is prepared for this 
Project.  We have the experience and resources required 
to provide you recommendations to meet budget re-
straints, lower maintenance issues, and provide a com-
fortable, productive building for years into the future. 
Our key staff members will remain dedicated to this Pro-
ject until final project completion.  NGA does not turn 
over staff frequently; in fact the average length of em-
ployment with our firm is 25 years.  With that being said, 
we feel comfortable saying: the Design Team that starts 
this endeavor with you will be the same people that are 
there with you at substantial completion.   
NGA has worked with RJA previously on 3 projects.  NGA 
has worked with the other members of this Design Team   
for over 5 years, some relationships extending beyond 
their current firms.

Reilly Johnson Architecture (RJA) is an architectural 
firm located in downtown Denver.  Since 1988, RJA 
has specialized in the planning and design of Justice 
Architecture.  Since that time, the Firm has built its 
practice around the design of Justice Facilities in-
cluding: Courts, Sherriff and Police Law Enforcement, 
Adult and Juvenile Detention and Corrections, Dis-
trict Attorney and Probation offices.  
RJA is recognized as a leader in the region for Justice 
Architecture, having planned and/or designed justice 
projects in Wyoming, Colorado, Montana, New Mex-
ico, Nebraska, and Iowa.  The Firm has been respon-
sible for planning and designing projects from small-
er scale additions to the Master Planning, Concept 
Design, and Total Project Cost modeling of the 
$378M Denver Justice Center, comprised of 1,500-
bed Pre-sentenced Detention Center, a Criminal and 
Juvenile Courts building with 35 court sets, and a 
450 space parking structure with a United States 
Post Office.  
RJA has a staff of ten, including five registered archi-
tects, three graduate/intern architects, one CAD 
drafter and one administrative assistant.  Most of 
our staff have extensive experience with courts, law 
enforcement,  detention, corrections, District Attor-
ney and Probation projects.  The three Principals in 
the firm have an average of over 30 years experience 
each with courts, detention, corrections, and law 
enforcement projects.  All of our registered archi-
tects also have expertise in the planning, design, 
and/or construction administration of detention/
corrections and/or courts.  They have been involved 
in every phase of project delivery, including: space 
requirements, programming, master planning, Total 
Project Cost Modeling, concept design, existing facil-
ity review and analysis, design development, site 
design, production of construction documents, con-
struction administration, and project close-out.  
RJA has an extensive list of courts and detention/
corrections projects.  And, over half of these projects 
involve major additions to, and remodeling of, ex-
isting facilities that must remain fully operational 
during what is often carefully planned phased con-
struction that allows for the continued safety of the 
public, staff, and inmates. 
RJA provides Long Term Facility Needs Assessments, 
Feasibility Studies, Site Analysis, Master Planning, 
Programming of Space Requirements, Concept De-
sign, Preliminary Total Project Cost Modeling, Archi-
tecture, and Interior Design services for Justice Ar-
chitecture.

Noel Griffith, Jr. AIA 
Noel will serve as your Principal Architect and will lead and orchestrate the 
Project Team throughout your project by assisting to better define project 
program and design.  He will communicate with client, consultant, contrac-
tor, and permitting agencies.  Noel will also review pay request and con-
struction documents, as well as oversee project budget and schedule.  No-
el’s client-centered focus has always been to provide a functional, respon-
sible facility that meets or exceeds his clients' project goals.  He accom-
plishes this by implementing decades of design and construction experi-
ence into every aspect of his projects.   His experience is wide ranging and 
touches nearly every corner of the profession.  Noel was the project archi-
tect on the 1995 Laramie County Government Building.

Robert Johnson 
Associate Architect, Lead Programmer | Planner 
Mr. Johnson has over 35 years experience as a Principal in Charge and/or 
Project Manager for master planning, programming, and design. For al-
most 30 years, he has focused on the planning and design of Justice facili-
ties.   
He has been with Reilly Johnson Architecture since 1985, and was named a 
principal in 1987, and president of the firm in 1998. From 1978 to 1985 Mr. 
Johnson was with RNL Design of Denver, where he was a project manager 
for planning/programming, architecture, and interiors projects for both 
private and public sector clients.   
Since 1988 he has been continuously involved in the planning of courts, 
law enforcement, detention, and corrections projects for municipal, coun-
ty, and state clients throughout Colorado, Wyoming, Montana, Nebraska, 
Iowa, and New Mexico, among other things, he is extremely skilled in mas-
ter planning, space requirements programming, total project cost model-
ing, and concept design. 
In recent few years he was Principal in Charge of the master planning, pro-
gramming, and concept design for the new 1.2M sf, $370M downtown 
Denver, CO Justice Center for both Mayor Webb and Mayor Hickenlooper; 
and for the 586,000 sf, Jefferson County, CO Sheriff Complex 2025 Master-
plan.

29 
B 
Schedule 
Section 
B 
Time is of the essence given the deadline for relo-
cation of the Municipal Courts by February 1, 
2018.  Therefore, we propose to get an “early 
start” on the Project by beginning work with the 
City on September 15th (about one month prior to 
the final execution/approval of the Design Services 
Agreement).  With this early start, we can assist 
with the CMAR selection process (have CMAR can-
didates selected for interviews), have completed 
our Scoping Meeting, and  have reviewed Concept 
Design options for the Temporary Relocation all 
prior to the October 11th date.  We will accomplish 
these tasks and refrain from sending our first in-
voice to the City until after our contract is fully-
executed.   
During the Scoping Meeting we wish to identify all 
the City’s objectives, both operational and finan-
cial, that they may be in addition to the primary 
purpose of relocation the Municipal Courts to 
2101 O’Neil.  
Our Design Team has already started to examine 
2101 O’Neil in terms of how and where the Addi-
tion(s) may be placed on the building, and how 
and where we might temporarily relocate the Mu-
nicipal Court.  Through our initial investigation, we 
are confident that our Design Team has a very use-
ful preliminary understanding of what it will take 
to meet, or exceed, the Court’s minimum needs in 
its Temporary Relocation.  Our Team will be very 
prepared to “hit the ground running”.   
As shown on the Municipal Court Temporary Relo-
cation (MCTR) Early Start Schedule on the follow-
ing page, we would also propose that the CMAR 
be allowed to have an “early start” on the Project 
prior to the approval and signing of its Agreement 
with the City.   If that arrangement would be ac-
ceptable, we would have about 2 weeks to work 
with the CMAR before we would complete the 
Construction Documents for the temporary reloca-
tion.   
In addition to advising the Design Team during the 
preparation of Design and Construction Docu-
ments for the temporary relocation, this “early 
start” by the CMAR could well have the added 
benefit of allowing the CMAR to have completed 
its Schematic Design Cost Estimate for the Addi-
tion|Renovation a day, or two, before the City 
would have to approve of the CMAR’s construc-
tion cost for the Temporary Remodel.  This way 
the City’s approval of the cost of construction for 
the Temporary Relocation is made in the context 
of the preliminary cost estimates of the Addi-
tion|Renovation 
The MCTR Early Start Schedule on the following 
page allows approximately 47 days for construc-
tion of the temporary relocation.  While this is an 
unusually short duration, our Design Team will 
provide a design that will not only strive to mini-
mize costs, but will also be possible to construct 
within this time frame.  And, the CMAR will have 
been advised of this Schedule prior to selection, 
and will not be surprised if it finds it has to staff 
the job more aggressively to allow completion by 
January 29th. 
The proposed schedule for your Addition and/or 
Renovation (A|R) Project is also provided in this 
Section.  The anticipated or desired end date of 
the overall A|R Project was not defined in the RFP, 
and the proposed A|R Schedule can be slightly 
modified to accommodate the City as needed. 
Our Design Team has extensive experience main-
taining and improving project schedules.  One of 
our methods is to expedite the Plan Review by 
working with those authorities early in the prelimi-
nary/schematic design.  They will be aware of our 
design intent before final submittal to ensure the 
design concept is agreeable prior to minor issues 
becoming roadblocks.    
The following pages include the MCTR Early Start 
Schedule, Exhibit C which demonstrates the over-
all schedule that includes the A|R portion of the 
work, and a graphic at the bottom of pages 30 and 
31 that illustrate the schedule overlaps.  At the 
end of this Section you will find the NGA and RJA’s 
projected work load and a chart that illustrates the 
percentage of team member participation. 
Rio Blanco County Justice Center—Reilly Johnson Architecture

35 
C 
Project Area 
Section 
C 
As shown in Section E References, and expanded 
upon in Section F Other Information, the NGA|RJA 
Design Team has extensive experience that will 
provide useful information for you to increase 
function in your new location and provide you 
great value for your dollar. 
The NGA|RJA Team is very familiar with this Pro-
ject in particular, including the specific aspects 
that will present opportunities as well as obstacles 
and challenges during the design and construction 
of this Project.  Some of these are discussed in this 
section. 
FAMILIAR WITH THE EXISTING 
COURT FACILITY 
NGA was the project architect for the original 
court building in 1995.  We have intimate 
knowledge of why the building was designed the 
way it was and the correlation of the different 
components.  NGA|RJA toured the Municipal 
Courts suite on August 21 to re-acquaint them-
selves with it and to verify the current uses and 
sizes of the various spaces within the suite.  The 
total usable floor area of the Municipal Court suite 
on the 2nd floor is 7,525 sf.  This figure excludes 
the exit stair on the east exterior wall and it ex-
cludes about 250 sf of Storage in the Basement of 
the building.  The two courtrooms are 1,000 sf 
each.  The Clerk’s Office is approximately 2,400 sf, 
including a 192 sf high-density File Storage Room, 
Staff Breakroom, and Staff Toilet.  There are cur-
rently three (3) judges chambers, one of which is 
the Jury Deliberation Room.  These chambers are 
236 sf, 245 sf, and 270sf. 
We understand that court programming has 
evolved over the last 20 years and have sugges-
tions on areas of improvement for your new facili-
ty.  We are very interested to receive your input 
on what portions of the current design has worked 
well with the way our City’s Municipal Courts op-
erate today and what areas can be improved upon 
to increase the function of operations for the fu-
ture.   
 
EXTENSIVE COURTROOM              
EXPERIENCE 
The NGA|RJA Design Team includes the expertise 
of Reilly Johnson Architecture and HJSA, our 
Courtroom Technology consultant.  With their 
extensive experience in the planning and design 
of courts facilities our Design Team is well 
equipped to provide all the planning, program-
ming, and design expertise needed for this Pro-
ject.  RJA and HJSA have worked together on 
many courts projects in Wyoming and elsewhere 
in the Rocky Mountain Region. 
This special expertise of RJA and HJSA will not be 
limited to the Programming, Schematic Design, 
and Design Development Phases, but will contin-
ue in the preparation of Construction Documents 
and through Construction of both the Temporary 
Relocation of the Municipal Court and its perma-
nent home in the Addition and/or Renovation. 
NGA and RJA have spent considerable time ex-
ploring various solutions to the Temporary Relo-
cation  prior to this Proposal Response and we are 
confident that we have some ideas that will satis-
fy the Court and ensure making the February 1st 
deadline. 
Our Design Team’s Court Programming|Design 
experience is illustrated in Section E References.  
Pictured: Johnson County Judicial Center  
Reilly Johnson Architecture  

noel griffith jr & associates architects | Reilly Johnson Architecture 
 
OTHER RELEVANT EXPERIENCE 
Our Design Team has extensive relevant experience 
pertinent to several aspects of your Project, which 
again is illustrated in Section F Other Information.  Our 
experience in projects that have elevated security 
needs and highly technical requirements will ensure 
the details are incorporated correctly into your Pro-
ject.  Our Design Team’s experience with the CMAR 
delivery method will provide the necessary team envi-
ronment that your Project will need for a successful 
completion.    With a combined amount of over 500 
renovation/addition projects completed, the NGA|RJA 
Design Team is well versed in this arena and will have 
the foresight and experience to combat the obstacles 
this type of project may present. 
FAMILIAR WITH THE EXISTING CITY 
GOVERNEMENT BUILDING  
With the objective of relocating the Municipal Court  
to the City Government Building either as an addition 
or incorporated as a renovation, an in-depth 
knowledge of 2101 O’Neil will be vital for project suc-
cess. 
Our Design Team is intimately familiar with the ex-
isting City Building.  With over 40 years conducting 
business in Cheyenne, including volunteering for the 
Licensing Board for the past 23 years, attending nu-
merous City Council Meetings, and visiting nearly eve-
ry square inch of the building, NGA has a solid under-
standing of some of the opportunities and challenges 
this facility faces.   
Some of the challenges include: 
• 
ADA/ANSI compliance 
• 
Security needs 
• 
Parking impacts 
• 
North parking lot drainage assessment 
•

•

•

• 
Settling concerns 
•

Some of these issues will need to be considered when 
the Municipal Courts are integrated into 2101 O’Neil, 
either as an addition or a renovation.  We will advise 
you as to which of these elements will need to be ad-
dressed depending on the concept of the Courts inte-
gration into the building.  
One of the main issues that can effect Project budget 
and schedule is Asbestos Abatement.  We recommend 
that the City start asbestos investigation/testing im-
mediately to avoid adverse schedule and budget im-
pacts later.  
There are some opportunities and advantages as 
well, such as, the possibility to maximize functionali-
ty in the integration of the Courts with better utiliza-
tion of space and possibly offer additional space for 
the occupants of 2101 O’Neil as the design presents.  
Other opportunities may include to improve the visi-
tor experience as well as the main entry function to 
serve both the Courts and the City as well as Council. 
NEEDS AND OBJECTIVES 
Our Design Team understands  how valuable floor 
space is, especially when you are City Government.  
Not only does floor space cost tax payer money, it 
also needs to last well into the future without major 
improvements/modifications being required that will 
again use tax payer dollars.  City Government offic-
ing needs to stand the test of time and offer flexibil-
ity  for future needs.  Although more of a dedicated, 
specialized space, the Court is no exception to this 
rule.   
As many of you are aware, our Municipal Courts as 
we know them (being located in the County build-
ing), have features and amenities that may be ele-
vated further than what the needs of our Municipal 
Courts dictate; other elements will be essential and 
need to remain an integral aspect of this Project.  
During our Scoping Meeting we will discuss with you 
some of these items, examples include: the level of 
finishes/materials, furniture, dedicated jury areas, 
and courtroom technology. 
Some of these items will translate differently from 
the MCTR Project to the overall A|R Project.  This 
will be valuable information when planning the 
MCTR Project to minimize the cost and duration of 
the temporary relocation construction work.   
There will be several other items we will want to 
discuss with you in the Scoping Meeting that will 
Logan County Justice Center, Reilly Johnson Architecture

C 
Project Area 
focus on your needs and specific objectives for 
both the MCTR and the A|R Project.  We will dis-
cuss a few of them here to plant the seeds for fu-
ture discussions.    
One of the functional aspects to consider is wheth-
er there is a need to create a multi-use courtroom.  
One that will accommodate not only the Municipal 
Courts but also City Council, serve as a city em-
ployee meeting space or city employee training.  
Each of these functions will require their own set 
of requirements.  Some of these requirements will 
overlap, others will need to be considered in the 
design phase so they can be incorporated without 
major impacts later. 
Another aspect that we would like to explore with 
you is the area of technology advancements.  Our 
Design Team is well versed in court technology 
and want to review your current technology needs 
as well as discuss what some of your future tech-
nology needs may be.  We have fundamental and 
creative ways to accomplish your technology goals 
for both your temporary relocation as well as your 
future permanent location.  
The impact the Courts will have on the existing 
occupants of the City Building will also need to be 
discussed.  Our goal is for the Court addition to our 
City Building to have a minimal impact on the cur-
rent departments and visiting community, and 
provide improvements to the existing facility and 
functions where possible.  We will want to gather 
information from you regarding any current or 
future needs the City may have in regards to 2101 
O’Neil.  The Courts will bring their need for screen-
ing/security to the City Building, but is a higher 
level of security warranted for the City Building as 
a whole? What are the peak loads for both the 
Courts and the other City departments?  These 
and other areas for discussion will help us mini-
mize the Court’s relocation impact and provide 
information needed for the implementation of 
their relocation. 
There are many other elements to discuss, but 
that is what the Design Scoping Meeting is for.  We 
will be prepared for that meeting with you and 
start the full realization of your Project. 
Our Design Team’s initial objectives coincide with 
several of the objectives spelled out in the RFP.  
We will research ideas that will improve cost and 
schedule as we understand that the $9 million 
budget is all inclusive and time is money.  We will 
investigate several design options for the different 
opportunities for the location of the Courts.  We 
will discuss with you the pros and cons of each 
location and the impact each location will have on 
budget, schedule, and its impact on the existing 
City Building.  We will focus our initial efforts on 
accomplishing the February 1st Temporary Reloca-
tion deadline while concurrently evaluating the 
details of this temporary relocation on the overall 
project goals.   
We will do what we always do and that is provide 
you a full design effort.  Our Design Team will eval-
uate each aspect of your Project and provide you a 
full design that encompasses your Project needs 
and objectives.  We will provide you a complete, 
detailed set of construction drawings, and our full 
Design Team support throughout your Project. 
We will also assist with the selection and procure-
ment of a qualified CMAR.  Both NGA and RJA have 
experience in this process and will provide our as-
sistance and recommendations where needed.  
We excel in a team environment and will embrace 
this CMAR opportunity with you. 
Although we have extensive experience and famili-
arity with every aspect of your project, we don’t 
want to pretend that we know what you want for 
this Project down to every detail—but we look for-
ward to finding out!  While we have been working 
tirelessly on your Project thus far to identify poten-
tial challenges and opportunities, we know there is 
still more to learn from you, and we look forward 
to that opportunity.

The NGA |RJA Design Team commits to providing 
all of the Services defined in Exhibit A of the 
City’s RFP, with the following elaboration. 
First, we feel it is necessary - because of the Feb-
ruary 1, 2018 deadline to have the Municipal 
Court relocated into 2101 O’Neil - that our Design 
Team offer to begin its services as soon as we are 
notified of selection on, or about, September 
14th.  We would do so without having to send any 
invoices to the City until such time as the Profes-
sional Services Agreement is fully executed and 
the City issues its formal Notice-To-Proceed on 
October 11, 2017. 
We would wish to begin immediately with as-
sisting the City with the writing of the RFP for the 
CMAR.  We would be glad to share with the City 
many of the RFPs that have been used by NGA 
and RJA on other projects to possibly improve up-
on and expedite the issuance of this RFP. 
Secondly, we would like to begin with the Scoping 
Meeting, including the detailed Space Require-
ments programming for the Municipal Court for 
both its Temporary Relocation, and its permanent 
home in the Addition|Renovation.  For the pur-
poses of Space Requirements Programming, we 
would like to consider establishing the Space Re-
quirements for years 2020, 2030, and 2040 to al-
low a reasonable long-term horizon for this plan-
ning and design. 
During this Scoping Meeting, we would also like to 
discuss whether the City has other objectives for 
improving or increasing space for any of the cur-
rent or expected new occupants of the building.  
This will be relevant in that this Project may affect 
areas of the building beyond just that of the new 
location of the Courts. Your future goals for the 
building would need to be considered in this Pro-
ject in the event that those considerations are 
affected by this work completed in this Project.  
Some initial considerations may be whether there 
is 
need 
for 
upgrades 
of 
existing 
HVAC 
(heating|ventilating|air conditioning) to increase 
occupant comfort or energy efficiency; or Toilet 
Rooms and Elevators to be code compliant.  
Within a few days of these Scoping|Programming 
Meetings, our Design Team would prepare a Total 
Project Cost Model identifying all the “hard” and 
“soft” costs for the Project to establish what will 
be possible within the $9M Budget. 
With this mid-September start, we would also like 
to concurrently begin developing design concepts 
for both the Temporary Relocation and the Addi-
tion|Renovation. 
To further expedite the design for the Temporary 
Relocation we would consider it very necessary 
for the City and the County to reach an agreement 
about the current office furniture used by Munici-
pal Court.  From our understanding, this furniture 
is owned by the County and we would advise the 
City of the importance of discussing with the 
County whether the County will “give” this furni-
ture to the City, or “sell“ it to the City at an attrac-
tive price.  It would be extremely helpful if this 
question could be resolved with the City by Sep-
tember 20th so that our Design Team knows how 
to proceed with the planning for the Temporary 
Relocation. 
Assuming the County will “give” or “sell” the furni-
ture, our Design Team would immediately inven-
tory all this furniture.  Then we can properly plan 
for its move to the Temporary Relocation and de-
velop design concepts for the Temporary Reloca-
tion that clearly demonstrate the space is able to 
accommodate this furniture, as well as the file 
cabinets and other things that may be owned by 
the City. 
If this furniture is not going to be available for the 
move to 2101 O’Neil, then the City can ask our 
Design Team to provide Additional Services (if 
such services are not available from City staff or 
furniture dealers) to specify furniture that can be 
delivered and installed by the February 1st dead-
line.  Obviously, the ability to take the Court’s cur-
rent furniture to 2101 O’Neil would greatly simpli-
fy meeting the February 1st deadline. 
Regarding the design effort for the Addi-
tion|Renovation, our Design Team is keenly aware 
of the challenges of where to build the Addition(s) 
to the building.  Our Concept Design effort will 
look at all the possible locations, including: on the 
north end of the building, on the west side of the 
building, and the east side (landscaped plaza) of 
the building, and any combination thereof.  We 
will want to involve the CMAR immediately in the 
critique of any of these concepts in terms of both 
constructability and costs, therefore, we hope the 
CMAR can begin providing limited services prior to 
a fully-executed Agreement with the City. 

noel griffith jr & associates architects | Reilly Johnson Architecture 
If possible, our Design Team would like to utilize 
“design charrettes” or “On-Site Design Sessions” for 
the planning and design of both the Temporary Reloca-
tion and the Addition|Renovation.  Ideally, we could 
find a room in 2101 O’Neil for such use.  One possibil-
ity for such a room may be the meeting room on the 
south side of Council Chambers.  This room is nicely 
sized for allowing many members of our Design Team 
to be working together, in your building, where City 
staff (Mayor, Council, Public Works, Court) would have 
relatively convenient access to us during the day, 
whenever they would have a spare moment, or two, to 
come interact with us.  If locating these design char-
rettes within the City Building is impractical or incon-
venient for you, that is not a problem, we will hold 
these design charrettes in our (Noel’s) office and invite 
you to join. 
We would likely have multi-day “charettes” and 
“worksessions” beginning with the Scoping Meeting, 
and continuing through the Design Development phase 
for the Addition|Renovation.  For the multi-day ses-
sions we often have a meeting at 4:00, or so, at the 
end of the day to review the day’s discoveries, ideas, 
progress.  We often work into the evening (10:00 pm, 
or later), and therefore, have meetings the following 
morning to review the previous night’s work. 
For the benefit of the Temporary Relocation, we would 
hope that the CMAR could participate in these sessions 
almost immediately upon their selection, without hav-
ing to wait for the full-execution of their Agreement 
with the City.  As quickly as we can develop design con-
cepts the CMAR can begin their critique of such con-
cepts for “constructability” and costs. 
Both NGA and RJA have found on past projects that 
this “on-site” charrette approach really accelerates the 
rate at which information is exchanged between us 
and our clients, and deepens the Design Team’s under-
standing of our client’s goals.  We would expect a total 
of 4 or 5 of these “On-Site Design Sessions” through 
the Schematic Design of the Temporary Relocation, 
and through the Design Development phase of the 
Addition|Renovation. 
And, while the RFP requests bi-monthly meetings with 
the City through the Design phase (Schematic Design + 
Design Development) we anticipate several months 
where we will have more than just two (2) meetings.  
Reilly Johnson Architecture, with their expertise in 
Court Programming|Design, will lead the Design Team 
in the Schematic Design and Design Development 
efforts and will be at most, if not all, the meetings with 
the City through the Design Development phase.  RJA 
will continue to be an integral part of the Design Team 
in the preparation of Construction Documents, assur-
ing continuality of design intent.   
Regarding interaction with the CMAR during the de-
sign phases of the Addition|Renovation, we would 
suggest that the CMAR services include their prepara-
tion of both a Schematic Design and a Design Develop-
ment construction cost estimate (Statement of Proba-
ble Cost), and that each of those estimates is reviewed 
in the context of the Design Team’s estimates and 
then discussed with the City. 
Also, prior to the CMAR preparation we would pro-
pose that the City ask the CMAR to conduct a “Job 
Fair|Project Open House” inviting all interested sub-
contractors and suppliers to attend, in hopes of not 
only increasing “local” participation in the Project, but 
also knowing that increasing such interest will result in 
better “participation” in the Project and consequently 
better pricing.  Our Design Team would attend such a 
function to present drawings for the Project, and to 
answer questions from subcontractors and suppliers. 
We also foresee the need to prepare a GMP Pricing 
Package to be given to the CMAR for preparation of its 
Guaranteed Maximum Price (GMP).  The CMAR’s GMP 
will be carefully reviewed by the Design Team, and 
discussed with the City, and it will likely be revised, at 
least once, before the Design Team would be satisfied 
that it should be presented to the City, with a recom-
mendation from the Design Team that it be accepted 
or approved.   
Our efforts will continue through Construction as we 
will attend the weekly OAC (Owner-Architect-
Contractor) meetings and we will be readily available 
to meet on-site to answer any questions or address 
any issues that may arise outside of the those sched-
uled meetings.  We will also attend City Council and 
Finance Committee Meetings as necessary.   
Members of our Design team will stay committed to 
your Project until Project Completion and beyond, as 
we understand the importance of this Project to our 
community.

Reilly Johnson Architecture was Associate Architect to GSG Architecture of Casper, Wyoming for 30,900sf courts 
facility that was completed in 2012 and for the Law Enforcement + Detention Center completed in 2011.   
This two story courts facility accommodates Circuit Court on the first floor and District Court on the second.  Each 
court consists of an 1,800 sf jury trial courtroom, jury deliberation suite, offices for the Clerk of court, judges cham-
bers, and courts staff offices.  The first floor also includes a 500 sf conference law library that will double as a juve-
nile hearing room, 2,700 sf office suite for the County Attorney, and public entry lobby.  Both courtrooms are con-
nected to the Detention Center via an at grade inmate movement corridor, with secure hold areas provided direct-
ly adjacent to each.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
This Project involved planning and design for new County and District Courts, Probation, District Attorney, and 
Sheriff Law Enforcement Office, Dispatch/911/Emergency Operations Center, and Detention Center.  
The Project used an abandoned red sandstone elementary school that was across the street from the historic 
Courthouse in downtown Meeker.  The elementary school was built in 1937 as one of the last projects constructed 
by the federal WPA (Works Progress Administration).  
About 15,000 sf of the original elementary school was used, along with an Addition to that school of approximately 
3,000 sf to accommodate County and District Courts, Probation, and District Attorney.  Another 23,000 sf of new 
construction was attached to this “courts” building to accommodate the Sheriff’s operations. 
Space Requirements Programming for this Project began in late 2013, and construction finished in early 2016.  The 
construction contract was negotiated with a CM|GC (Construction Manager | General Contractor) having a Guar-
anteed Maximum Price (GMP). 
The Site Plan below, shows the historic Courthouse on the left (west) and the Justice Center incorporating the ele-
mentary school on the right (east).  The Project included the closing of a public street between these two buildings 
to increase the public green space, and it also included a new outdoor pavilion/bandstand, and extensive landscap-
ing.   
After the Courts and Sheriff moved from the historic Courthouse to the new Justice Center, RJA was retained by 
the County to expand and remodel of the historic Courthouse for the County’s administrative offices and IT, as well 
as the County’s broadband facilities.  That 35,000 sf remodel project completed construction in 2017 and it was 
also delivered via a CM|GC contract having a GMP.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
Reilly Johnson Architecture (RJA) began working with Fremont County in 2009 with the preparation of a 
Needs Assessment and a Space Requirements Program for a new Riverton Justice Center to replace their 
severely overcrowded and obsolete facility. 
This 15,000 gsf new facility was to include what would be called “satellite” facilities for Circuit Court, Coun-
ty & Prosecuting Attorney, and Sherriff Law Enforcement offices.  These facilities are satellite to all these 
functions that are also located in the County seat of Lander, approximately 24 miles away. 
In 2012, RJA conducted a “test fit” and Building Condition Assessment on four existing buildings in River-
ton.  Upon review of those efforts, the County decided building a new building, on a new County-owned 
site would best serve these uses. 
This new Justice Center houses the Circuit Court of the Ninth Judicial District, the District Attorney, and the 
Fremont County Sheriff’s Department.  Circuit Court includes one jury trial courtroom, chambers, jury de-
liberation, and offices for the Clerk of Court.  The 2,600 sf District Attorney’s suite accommodates a staff of 
12.  The 5,500 sf Sheriff’s Department includes office space for 16, a vehicle sallyport, court holding cells 
adjacent to the courtroom, and evidence storage. 
The Project completed construction in Spring 2015.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
Reilly Johnson Architecture served as courts consultant and was Associate Architect to GSG Architecture, and the 
National Center for State Courts, in the preparation of a Long Term Facility Needs Analysis, Space Requirements 
Program, Facility Master Plan, and Concept Design for an addition and renovation to the existing Natrona County 
Courthouse in Casper.  Phase One of the Master Plan included the addition of a new secure Public Lobby, three 
District Courtrooms, Judges Chambers, Offices for the Clerk of District Court, and Jury Deliberation Suites.  The 
basement housed a vehicle sallyport, inmate holding, contact visiting rooms, and secure courts-staff parking.  In 
addition, the existing building was to be remodeled to allow for much needed expansion of existing County func-
tions.  Phase Two added Circuit and Municipal Courts into the new courthouse—both then located in the adjacent 
Hall of Justice building.  
After two failed bond issues, the County purchased an historic hotel, The Townsend Building, located directly 
across the street from the existing County Courthouse.  In 2006 RJA and GSG began detailed design on a new 
$21.6 million, 101,200 sf facility (as shown above), that includes a building addition that houses four District Court-
rooms, a vehicle sallyport, and below grade inmate holding.  The existing five story Townsend Building was remod-
eled to house three Circuit Court “sets”, Chambers, offices for the Clerks of Court, a Juvenile Hearing Room, and 
Records/Evidence Storage.

PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
The Reilly Johnson Architecture team provided programming, master planning, 
and concept design services for the new Denver Justice Center, from 2002 
through a successful Ballot Question regarding financing in 2005.  Construction 
completed on these three buildings in 2010.  This 1.1 million square-foot project 
includes a Courts Building, a Pre-sentenced Detention Center, and a separate 
Parking Structure and United State Post Office, all located on two and a half 
blocks in downtown Denver. 
The Courts Building provides 35 courtrooms for the District Court criminal divi-
sion, County Court criminal division, Drug Court and Juvenile Court.  The court-
room design provides separate circulation for the public, judges and staff, and 
inmates.  A secure inmate holding area is provided in the basement of the Courts 
Building. 
The Detention Center will lessen the overcrowding at the existing Smith Road 
County Jail and replace the existing Pre-Arraignment Detention Facility (City Jail).  
The Detention Center includes 1,500 beds, program and exercise areas, medical, 
intake, administration, kitchen, and laundry.  Two pre-arraignment courtrooms 
are included in the Detention Center, as is public video visitation.  A secure under-
ground corridor connects the Detention Center to the Courts Building.
