REILLY JOHNSON ARCHITECTURE (RJA) is a 36 year old architectural firm in 
Denver, that since 1988 has specialized in the planning, programming, and 
design of Justice Architecture. This is not one of many specializations within 
the Firm - it is the only specialization. RJA has planned, programmed, and 
designed: courts, district attorney offices, probation offices, crime labs, adult 
and juvenile detention and corrections facilities, and Sheriff and Police law 
enforcement offices throughout Colorado and Wyoming; and in Montana, 
Nebraska, New Mexico, Iowa, and Louisiana. 
RJA’s expertise in courts planning, programming, and design began in 1996 
when Rick Backes joined the Firm, as one of three principals. Mr. Backes 
had previous experience with major courts projects, including the 275,000 
sf United States Courthouse in Kansas City when he was with Gossen Living-
ston Associates of Wichita + Kansas City, Kansas. RJA’s first courts project in 
Colorado was the Logan County Justice Center in Sterling, Colorado which  
began planning in 2001 and in which construction was completed in 2003. 
Since that time, RJA has been fortunate to have provided courts planning and 
design throughout Colorado and Wyoming which has developed considera-
ble courts expertise on the part of - Robert Johnson, one of the Firm’s princi-
pals - and in Page Phillips, Associate Principal.

COST ESTIMATING 
The NGA|RJA Design Team is experienced in pre-
paring cost estimates.  Over the past few years the 
construction industry has been highly volatile in 
regards to construction costs; to combat this, our 
Design Team works directly with the construction 
industry to stay abreast of the latest fluctuations in 
construction costs.   Our Design Team implements 
estimating procedures that utilize our current local 
project actual bid costs to represent estimates re-
flective of the local bidding climate. 
WORKING RELATIONSHIPS 
NGA assembled this Design Team specifically for 
your Project.  Not only does every team member 
share the commitment to technical excellence as 
well as the drive and passion NGA strives to 
achieve, but they also have the experience and 
knowledge required to contribute to the success 
of your Project.   Every aspect of your Project will  
be considered in great detail, and each member of 
our Design Team has the skills needed to increase 
the functionality and success of your Project.   
A partnership is truly what NGA|RJA have.  We 
understand what is expected of each other and 
our firms compliment each other well.  RJA is ac-
customed with teaming with a local firm as that is 
the case in over half of their projects.  NGA has 
also teamed with specialized architectural firms in 
the past and has great experiences in the process.   
The NGA|RJA Design Team similarly has extensive 
experience completing projects with the CMAR 
delivery method.  We thrive in a team environ-
ment and having the CMAR be a part of the Pro-
ject Team is something we are well versed with.   
Our Design Team has also demonstrated abilities 
in working with a diverse group of individuals, such 
as: agencies, boards, elected officials, citizens, 
property owners, business, and various govern-
mental organizations.  We truly believe that the 
owner’s representative is a vital component to a 
project’s success, and value their input tremen-
dously.  To truly maximize the potential for the 
Cheyenne Municipal Courts we will have several 
investigative meetings with you, the owner, and 
our entire Design Team so that every design con-
sideration or element is thoroughly evaluated.  We 
have learned that putting the effort early in the 
design phase of the project results in highly satis-
factory outcomes later during construction. 
NO CONFLICTS 
The NGA|RJA Design Team does not have any 
known conflict of interest with the New Municipal 
Court Renovation and/or Addition Project. 
ADDITIONAL EXPERIENCE 
The following is a continuation of the brief sum-
mary of the vast and enormous experience profile 
our Design Team offers you.   NGA|RJA has assem-
bled a Design Team of experienced, dedicated, 
quality professionals whose focus and philosophy 
will be to provide the highest quality architectural 
and engineering services possible in a timely man-
ner by a staff committed to technical excellence 
and the highest integrity.   
In this Section, we will expand upon our experi-
ence with the other aspects of your Project, which 
include: elevated security needs, highly technical 
requirements, CMAR, Additions and/or Renova-
tions, Occupied Renovations, and 6th Penny Pro-
jects.

noel griffith jr & associates architects | Reilly Johnson Architecture 
RELOCATION OF STATE LEGISLATIVE BRANCH 
Negotiated Contract,  occupied A|R, Temporary Relocation 
 
 
CLIENT: State of Wyoming 
CONTACT REFERENCE 
Wendy Madsen or Anthony Sara 
Legislative Service Office 
(307) 777-7881  
YEAR COMPLETED: 2015 
PROJECT SIZE: 45,000 sf renovation  
PROJECT BUDGET: $750,000 
Completed within budget. 
CONTRACTOR: S&S Builders 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
The State of Wyoming commenced a complete renovation of the State Capitol Building, which required all oc-
cupants to be temporarily relocated during the multi-year project.  NGA was hired to provide design services to 
relocate the entire legislative branch to the Jonah Financial Building.  The design and construction had a tight 
time frame, with a defined move-out date at the Capitol Building.  A fast-track construction schedule was uti-
lized, with demolition and renovations beginning before final design drawings were complete.   
The project was a complex undertaking, given site constraints and the State’s unique requirements.  Facilities 
were needed for many different occupants and functions:  House and Senate chambers, galleries, lobbies, staff 
spaces, attorney spaces, and lounge and break rooms; committee rooms; hearing rooms; House and Senate 
leadership offices; substantial office and file spaces for the Wyoming Legislative Service Office; IT areas; media 
space; and state security accommodations.  Intense coordination of IT, security, and communications was re-
quired.  In addition, NGA also assisted in utilizing existing furniture provided by the Jonah Building ownership, 
which required substantial design consideration.  The project was completed within budget, and on time, so as 
not to delay commencement of the Capitol renovations.

SOCIAL SECURITY ADMINISTRATION 
Negotiated Contract,  occupied A|R, Heightened Security 
 
CLIENT: Pershing Circle LLC 
CONTACT REFERENCE 
Steve Sasser, Owner Representative 
307.235.5690 
YEAR COMPLETED: 2011 
PROJECT SIZE: 6,600 sf renovation  
PROJECT BUDGET: $544,000 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
This project included following specific design guidelines as set by the 
GSA while maintaining the unique programming needs of the Social 
Security Administration (SSA).  The existing space dictated the design 
of the new office floor plan, as the entire east wall, floor to ceiling, 
was glass.  Careful consideration was needed in the schematic phase 
as the SSA had special security needs.   
This project was completed in an occupied building successfully, and 
within the time requirements. 
C-130 SQUADRON OPERATION 
 Heightened Security 
 
CLIENT: Wyoming Air National Guard 
CONTACT REFERENCE 
Lt. Col. Tipple 307.772.6838 
YEAR COMPLETED: 2011 
PROJECT SIZE: 38,200 sf r 
PROJECT COST: $6 million 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
This state-of-the-art, highly-tactile, secure facility was 
one of the first buildings in the Air National Guard to 
achieve LEED Silver certification.  This project was com-
pleted within the construction schedule and within 
budget. 
This  Facility had unique and highly technical design re-
quirements throughout the entire 38,200 square-feet.  
This building housed highly-secure areas such as the 
Command Post and tactics and intelligence, which also 
required specialized design/construction techniques.  
The quality of the design of this building was of the ut-
most importance due to the high level of specialized 
functions this building incorporated; attention to detail 
was given to every aspect of this design.

PROJECT BUDGET: $2,383,000

REASON FOR VARIANCE: Unforeseen Existing Condi-
tions 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
This project involved three (3) separate construction phases; 
assisting the owner in phasing/scheduling asbestos abate-
ment, the interior renovation of the 5th floor, and the re-
roofing with parapet stabilization.  Although the 5th floor 
itself was vacated for the majority of these construction 
phases, all phases were completed while the remainder of 
the building remained occupied. 
C-130 FLIGHT SIMULATOR FACIILTY 
Heightened Security 
 
CLIENT: Wyoming Air National Guard 
CONTACT REFERENCE 
Lt. Col. Tripple, 307.772.6838 
YEAR COMPLETED: 2015 
PROJECT SIZE: 11,400 sf 
PROJECT BUDGET: $5M 
Project completed within budget 
CONTRACTOR: Faith Enterprises 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
This project involved the design of a building to 
house a Flight Simulator Training Facility for C-
130 aircraft. The secure facility consisted of offic-
es, classrooms, and simulator control/computer 
room, as well as the 58-foot by 51-foot by 40-
foot high flight simulator bay. This facility is con-
sidered ‘secure’. This facility is also accredited to 
Silver LEED status, and also incorporated the use 
of photo-voltaic (solar) panels on the roof.  The 
building was constructed as a design/bid project 
by Faith Enterprises, Inc. out of Colorado 
Springs, CO for $5 million. Construction was 
awarded in the Fall of 2013 and was completed 
in the fall of 2015

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_60250435.pdf

City Contract # __________ 
MPO Contract # 187446 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for the 
East Dell Range Boulevard / U.S. 30 Corridor Study - 2018 
 
PREAMBLE 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _____ day of _____________, 2018, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization, hereinafter referred 
to as the “MPO,” and Kimley Horn, hereinafter referred to as “Consultant.”  The City of Cheyenne 
is acting as the agent of the MPO for the contract administration and as the MPO’s Fiscal Manager 
in accordance with Contract #6111. 
 
AGREEMENT 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed in accordance with the project 
schedule attached hereto as Exhibit B, incorporated by reference and expressly made a part 
hereof.  Any extensions of the time limit set forth in the Scope of Work shall be agreed 
upon in writing between the parties. 
 
3. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
The Consultant shall be responsible for the professional quality, technical accuracy, timely 
completion of drawings and other services rendered by the Consultant and its sub-
consultants and shall, without additional compensation, promptly remedy and correct any 
errors, omissions, or other deficiencies, except to the extent that such errors, omissions or 
other deficiencies arise from the digital data created by Cheyenne/Laramie County 
Cooperative Geographic Information System (CLCCGIS). 
 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the 
same or similar locality. Upon notice to the Consultant and by mutual agreement between 
the parties, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not 
By BJR as to form only at 2:14 pm, May 16, 2018

 
2 
 
meeting such a standard. 
 
4. 
GIS MAPS AND DIGITAL DATA 
If the Consultant uses any maps, coverages, images, or other digital data created by the 
CLCCGIS for the project specified in Exhibit A, the Consultant agrees to return or destroy 
that information once the project is complete. Consultant agrees not to reuse or sell the GIS 
maps or data, which were provided as a professional courtesy and to minimize the cost of 
the project. 
 
5. 
GIS DATA LIMITATION AND DISCLAIMED LIABILITY 
GIS data is collected primarily for use by the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County. Any 
unauthorized use of the data is at the risk of the user.  
 
6. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
7. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants 
under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are 
required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance 
Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the 
Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the 
responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this 
policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure 
their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in 
violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
8. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term 
or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by 
the Consultant, or its employees or sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  
The Consultant acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant 
to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
9. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 

 
3 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or 
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily 
injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the entire term of the 
Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the 
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services 
under this Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ 
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the 
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; 
 
b. In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new 
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect 
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this 
Agreement; 
 
c. Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement; 
and  
 
d. In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior 
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this 
paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of 
the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for 
an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period 
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has 
provided services under this Agreement.  

 
4 
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne and the MPO as an Additional 
Insured by endorsement on its insurance policies, with the exception of workers’ 
compensation and professional liability insurance and shall provide the City with a copy 
of the endorsements. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that 
has resulted in or may result in the cancellation or substantive change of any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of 
insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional 
insured.  The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates 
shall not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
10. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES  49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex. 
 
11. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the MPO and the MPO agrees to pay Consultant a total amount of ninety-seven 
thousand, one hundred and forty-five Dollars ($97,145.00), in accordance with the fee 
schedule given in Exhibit C, which is attached to this Agreement, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. The amounts of all such payments shall be based upon 
the Consultant’s progress, verified by the MPO, in completing the work as described in 
Exhibits A, B, and C.  Final payment shall be made following acceptance of the work by 
the MPO. Original quality reproductions of all designs, plans, reports, specifications, 
drawings and other services rendered by the Consultant shall become the sole property of 
the MPO and shall be delivered to the MPO, immediately upon their preparation to the 
extent the MPO has satisfied its payment obligations to the Consultant. A portion of the 
payment may be held until any claims are resolved. 
 

 
5 
 
12. 
MPO REPRESENTATIVE 
Prior to commencement of work, the MPO will designate in writing, an MPO 
Representative who shall make, within the scope of his/her authority, all necessary 
decisions regarding the project. All requests for contract interpretations, field orders, 
contract modifications, change orders, and other clarification or instruction shall be 
directed to the MPO Representative. 
 
13. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the MPO Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Works, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of 
any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
14. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the MPO or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the MPO. 
 
15. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
16. 
APPROVAL OF PLANS 
The MPO’s approval of drawings, designs, plans, specifications, reports, and incidental 
work or materials shall not in any way relieve the Consultant of responsibility for the 
technical accuracy of the work. The MPO’s approval or acceptance of, or payment for, any 
of the services shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the MPO’s rights 
under this Agreement or any of its legal rights under statute or common law arising out of 
the performance of this Agreement. 
 
17. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
18. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default. In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
a. Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount; or 

 
6 
 
 
b. Treat the agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
19. 
TERMINATION BY MPO 
The MPO Representative may, without cause, by written notice within ten (10) days to the 
Consultant, terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the MPO’s 
convenience. Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
a. Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
b. Deliver to the MPO Representative within five (5) days all data, drawings, 
specifications, reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and 
materials as may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
c. In the event of termination for convenience, the MPO will pay the Consultant for 
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such 
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
20. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
MPO may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
21. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
22. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The State of Wyoming, the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT), the City 
of Cheyenne, and Laramie County, along with their officials and employees, do not waive 
their governmental or sovereign immunity by entering into this Agreement, and the MPO 
does not waive governmental immunity, except to the extent necessary for the parties to 
pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the agreement, and each 
specifically retains all immunities and defenses available to them as sovereigns or 
governmental entities pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other state law. 
Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions should 
not be constructed as a waiver of governmental or sovereign immunity. The parties agree 
that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly construed, either against or for 
either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity shall be 
construed in favor of governmental or sovereign immunity. The State of Wyoming, 
WYDOT, the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, and the MPO reserve all immunities and 

 
7 
 
defenses available under the Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, 
et. seq. 
 
23. 
ENVIRONMENTAL POLICY ACTS 
The Consultant agrees all activities under this Agreement will comply with the Clean Air 
Act, the Clean Water Act, the National Environmental Policy Act, and other related 
provisions of federal environmental protection laws, rules or regulations. 
 
24. 
HUMAN TRAFFICKING 
As required by 22 U.S.C. § 7104(g) and 2 C.F.R. Part 175, this Agreement may be 
terminated without penalty if a private entity that receives funds under this Agreement: 
 
a. Engages in severe forms of trafficking in persons during the period of time that the 
award is in effect; 
 
b. Procures a commercial sex act during the period of time that the award is in effect; 
or 
 
c. Uses forced labor in the performance of the award or sub-awards under the award. 
 
25. 
KICKBACKS 
The Consultant certifies and warrants that no gratuities, kickbacks, or contingency fees 
were paid in connection with this Agreement, nor were any fees, commissions, gifts, or 
other considerations made contingent upon the award of this Agreement. If the Consultant 
breaches or violates this warranty, Cheyenne MPO may, at its discretion, terminate this 
Agreement without liability to Cheyenne MPO, or deduct from the agreed upon price or 
consideration, or otherwise recover, the full amount of any commission, percentage, 
brokerage, or contingency fee. 
 
26. 
LIMITATIONS ON LOBBYING ACTIVITIES 
By signing this Agreement, the Consultant certifies and agrees that, in accordance with 
Public Law 101-121, payments made from a federal grant shall not be utilized by the 
Consultant or its sub-consultants in connection with lobbying Congressmen, or any other 
federal agency in connection with the award of a federal grant, contract, cooperative 
agreement, or loan. 
 
27. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The Cheyenne MPO shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement 
that are performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be 
limited to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to 
bring experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
 
 

 
8 
 
28. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., the Age Discrimination Act of 1975, 
and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto and shall not discriminate 
against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national 
origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection with the performance 
under this Agreement. 
 
29. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that 
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming. 
 
30. 
PUBLICITY 
Any publicity given to the program or services provided herein, including, but not limited 
to, notices, information, pamphlets, press releases, research, reports, signs, and similar 
public notices prepared by or for the Consultant and related to the services and work to be 
performed under this Agreement, shall identify Cheyenne MPO, WYDOT and/or the 
Federal Highway Administration as the sponsoring agency and shall not be released 
without prior written approval of Cheyenne MPO and WYDOT. 
 
31. 
SUSPENSION AND DEBARMENT 
By signing this Agreement, the Consultant certifies that neither it nor its principals are 
presently debarred, suspended, proposed for debarment, declared ineligible, or voluntarily 
excluded from participation in this transaction nor from federal financial or non-financial 
assistance, nor are any of the participants involved in the execution of this Agreement 
suspended, debarred, or voluntarily excluded by any federal department or agency in 
accordance with Executive Order 12549 (Debarment and Suspension) and 44 C.F.R. Part 
17, or are on the debarred vendors list at www.epls.gov. Further, the Consultant agrees to 
notify Cheyenne MPO by certified mail should it or any of its agents become debarred, 
suspended, or voluntarily excluded during the term of this Agreement. 
 
32. 
NO GOVERNMENT OBLIGATION TO THIRD PARTIES 
No Obligation by the Federal Government. 
 
a. The Consultant acknowledges and agrees that, notwithstanding any concurrence by 
the Federal Government in or approval of the solicitation or award of the underlying 
contract, absent the express written consent by the Federal Government, the Federal 
Government is not a party to this Agreement and shall not be subject to any 
obligations or liabilities to the Consultant, or any other party (whether or not a party 
to that contract) pertaining to any matter resulting from the underlying contract. 
 
b. The Consultant agrees to include the above clause in each subcontract financed in 
whole or in part with Federal assistance provided by the Federal Transit 

 
9 
 
Administration (FTA).  It is further agreed that the clause shall not be modified, 
except to identify the sub-consultant who will be subject to its provisions. 
 
33. 
PROGRAM FRAUD AND FALSE OR FRAUDULENT STATEMENTS AND 
RELATED ACTS 
a. The Consultant acknowledges that the provisions of the Program Fraud Civil 
Remedies Act of 1986, as amended, 31 U.S.C. § 3801, et seq., and U.S. DOT 
regulations, “Program Fraud Civil Remedies,” 49 C.F.R. Part 31, apply to its 
actions pertaining to this Project. Upon execution of the underlying contract, the 
Consultant certifies or affirms the truthfulness and accuracy of any statement it has 
made, it makes, it may make, or causes to be made, pertaining to the underlying 
contract or the FTA assisted project for which this contract work is being 
performed. In addition to other penalties that may be applicable, the Consultant 
further acknowledges that if it makes, or causes to be made, a false, fictitious, or 
fraudulent claim, statement, submission, or certification, the Federal Government 
reserves the right to impose the penalties of the Program Fraud Civil Remedies Act

b. The Consultant also acknowledges that if it makes, or causes to be made, a false,

U.S.C. § 1001 and 49 U.S.C. § 5307(n)(1) on the Consultant, to the extent the 
Federal Government deems appropriate. 
 
c. The Consultant agrees to include the above two clauses in each sub-contract 
financed in whole or in part with Federal assistance provided by FTA.  It is further 
agreed that the clauses shall not be modified, except to identify the sub-consultant 
who will be subject to the provisions. 
 
34. 
FEDERAL CHANGES  49 C.F.R. Part 18 
Consultant shall at all times comply with all applicable FTA regulations, policies, 
procedures and directives, including without limitation those listed directly or by reference 
in the Agreement (Form FTA MA (18) dated October 2011) between consultant and FTA, 
as they may be amended or promulgated from time to time during the term of this 
Agreement.  Consultant’s failure to so comply shall constitute a material breach of this 
Agreement. 
 
35. 
INCORPORATION OF FEDERAL TRANSIT ADMINISTRATION (FTA) TERMS   
FTA Circular 4220.1F 
Applicability:  The incorporation of FTA terms applies to all contracts. 
The preceding provisions include, in part, certain Standard Terms and Conditions required

mandated terms shall be deemed to control in the event of a conflict with other provisions

36. 
LOBBYING  31 U.S.C. § 1352 – 49 C.F.R. Part 19 - 49 C.F.R. Part 20 
Byrd Anti-Lobbying Amendment, 31 U.S.C. § 1352, as amended by the Lobbying

of 1995 who has made lobbying contacts on its behalf with non-Federal funds with respect 
to that Federal contract, grant or award covered by 31 U.S.C. § 1352.  Such disclosures are

37.  
ADA ACCESSIBILITY  42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq.  
Applicability:  Facilities construction or renovation contracts. 
Facilities to be used in public transportation service must comply with 42 U.S.C. § 12101, 
et seq., and DOT regulations, “Transportation Services for Individuals with Disabilities 
(ADA),” 49 C.F.R. Part 37; and Joint ATBCB/DOT regulations, “Americans with

PRIVACY ACT  5 U.S.C. § 552 
Applicability:  The Federal Privacy Act requirements flow down to each third-party

to comply with the terms of the privacy Act may result in termination of the underlying

whole or in part with Federal assistance provided by FTA. 
 
39. 
MISCELLANEOUS TERMS REQUIRED BY WYOMING DEPARTMENT OF 
TRANSPORTATION 
 
a. Special Provisions 
 
(i) 
Conflict of Interest.  The subrecipient must disclose in writing any potential 
conflict of interest to WYDOT including financial or other personal 
interests.   
 
(ii) 
Mandatory Disclosures.  The subrecipient must disclose, in a timely 
manner, in writing to WYDOT all violations of Federal criminal law 
involving fraud, bribery, or gratuity violations potentially affecting this 
award.  Failure to make required disclosures can result in remedies for 
noncompliance including suspension or debarment. 
 
b. General Provisions 
 
(i) 
Amendments.  Either party may request changes in this Agreement.  Any 
changes, modifications, revisions or amendments to this Agreement which 
are mutually agreed upon by and between the parties to this Agreement shall 
be incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all parties to 
this Agreement. 
 
(ii) 
Assumption of Risk.  The City shall assume the risk of any loss of state or 
federal funding, either administrative or program dollars, due to its failure 
to comply with state or federal requirements. WYDOT will notify the City 
of any state or federal determination of noncompliance. 
 
(iii) 
Availability of Funds.  Each payment obligation of WYDOT is conditioned 
upon the availability of government funds which are appropriated or 
allocated for the payment of this obligation.  If funds are not allocated and 
available for the continuance of the services performed by the City, this 
Agreement may be terminated by WYDOT at the end of the period for 
which the funds are available.  WYDOT will notify the City at the earliest 
possible time of the services which will or may be affected by a shortage of 
funds.  No penalty shall accrue to WYDOT in the event this provision is 
exercised, and WYDOT will not be obligated or liable for any future 
payments due or for any damages as a result of termination under this 
section.  This provision shall not be construed to permit WYDOT to 

 
12 
 
terminate this Agreement in order to acquire similar services from another 
party. 
 
(iv) 
Notices.  All notices arising out of, or from, the provisions of this 
Agreement shall be in writing and given to the parties at the address 
provided under this Agreement, either delivered by hand or certified mail. 
 
(v) 
Prior Approval. This Agreement shall not be binding upon either party, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this Agreement, and the 
Wyoming State Auditor shall not draw warrants for payment on this 
Agreement until this Agreement has been reduced to writing, approved as 
to form by the Office of the Attorney General, and approved by the 
Governor of the State of Wyoming, or his designee, if required by Wyo. 
Stat. §9-2-1016(b)(iv). 
 
(vi) 
Severability.  Should any portion of this Agreement be judicially 
determined to be illegal or unenforceable, the remainder of this Agreement 
shall continue in full force and effect, and either party may renegotiate the 
terms affected by the severance. 
 
(vii) 
Assignment/Agreement Not Used as Collateral.  Neither party shall assign 
nor otherwise transfer any of the rights or delegate any of the duties set forth 
in this Agreement without the prior written consent of the other party. The 
City shall not use this Agreement, or any portion thereof, for collateral for 
any financial obligation without the prior written permission of WYDOT. 
 
40. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of 13 pages, and Exhibits A, B, and C consisting of 7, 1, and 1 
pages respectively, and submitted proposal with negotiated changes, contains the entire 
understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, 
concerning or controlling this matter. Time is of the essence hereof. Any timing extension 
shall be approved in writing by both parties. 
   
Notwithstanding the foregoing, the MPO Master Agreement (Contract #6111) is hereby 
incorporated into and made a part of this Agreement. This Agreement and the parties 
thereto shall be subject to the terms of the MPO Master Agreement, and in the event that 
the terms of the MPO Master Agreement conflicts with the terms of this Agreement, the 
terms of the MPO Master Agreement shall control. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunto set their hands and seals the day and year 
first above written.

EXHIBIT A 
Attached to Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming and Kimley-Horn 
for Planning and Engineering Services in Support of the East Dell Range and US 30 Corridor Study 

 
1 | P a g e 
 
Exhibit A 
Scope of Work 
for 
EAST DELL RANGE BOULEVARD/US 30 CORRIDOR STUDY 
 
Scope of Work 
 
TASK 1 – PROJECT MANAGEMENT, COORDINATION, AND COMMUNICATION  
The Kimley-Horn team will coordinate with the MPO, participate in team meetings and conference calls, 
and provide timely invoicing and reporting of the project’s progress. This effort will include an internal 
team kick-off meeting to identify key stakeholders, and to discuss expectations, coordination efforts, 
processes, and outcomes. 
 
Deliverable(s):  
• 
Monthly coordination meeting and documented action items  
• 
Monthly progress reporting and invoicing  
 
TASK 2 – PUBLIC KICKOFF AND GOAL SETTING  
Based feedback received through the community engagement activities, Kimley-Horn will draft a set of 
community-supported project goals and success measures for review and input from staff, the Project 
Steering Committee (PSC), and the community. These project goals and success measures will be used to 
guide the project’s mobility performance targets and objectives. 
 
Task 2.1 – Project Public Kick-off  
The Kimley-Horn team will attend and facilitate the project kick-off that will consist of the following 
activities: 
• 
Project Steering Committee (PSC) Meeting #1 – The Kimley-Horn team and MPO will hold a 
project kick-off meeting with the PSC to understand the Committee’s goals and objectives for the 
project. Participants at this meeting will: 
- 
Discuss expectations regarding project process, issues, concerns, schedule, scope refinement, 
outcomes, and products 
- 
Discuss conclusions from previous studies and coordinate efforts with current initiatives 
- 
Review existing materials and data requirements with the Project Team 
- 
Discuss schedule, roles, and responsibilities 
• 
Study Area Tour – Following the PSC meeting #1, the Kimley-Horn team will tour the plan area 
with key MPO, City staff, and project partners to discuss specific issues and opportunities related 
to the East Dell Range and US 30 corridors. The Kimley- Horn team will document the character 
of the study area through digital photographs of key locations and the surrounding 
neighborhoods. 
 
Task 2.2 – Focus Groups and Stakeholder Interviews  
Kimley-Horn will conduct up to eight (8) one-on-one interviews with elected officials, partner public 
agencies (Laramie County School District, Cheyenne Transit Program staff, and WYDOT), community 
leaders, property owners, development community representatives, neighborhood organizations, and 
other key stakeholders.  Kimley-Horn will conduct individual interviews to better understand the context 
of the area and specific expectations. The interviews will be brief (approximately one hour), informal, 
and designed to elicit issues and concerns from the stakeholders, as well as provide an opportunity to 
inform stakeholders about the project. These interviews will allow Kimley-Horn to discuss current 

 
2 | P a g e 
 
initiatives and available information; understand project expectations; and identify key issues that need 
to be addressed. 
 
Task 2.3 – Community Workshop #1 
Kimley-Horn will develop advertising materials and facilitate one, 2-hr evening public workshop which 
will introduce the project to the broader corridor community for MPO production and distribution. The 
intent of the workshop is to engage the public early in the process, while giving the Kimley- Horn team 
and MPO the ability to understand public issues, concerns, and objectives while learning the 
community’s key expectations for the corridor. 
 
Task 2.4 – Draft Project Goals and Success Measures  
Based on feedback received through the community engagement activities, Kimley-Horn will draft a series of 
community values, which will influence the study’s transportation performance targets, goals, and objectives. 
A draft set of project goals and anticipated measures of success will be developed for community review and 
confirmation. 
 
Deliverable(s):  
• 
Information for meeting announcements and advertising material for City publication and 
distribution 
• 
A memorandum summarizing PSC Meeting #1 
• 
A memorandum summarizing up to eight (8) stakeholder interviews 
• 
PowerPoint presentation for the Community Workshop #1 
• 
A memorandum summarizing Community Workshop #1 
• 
Draft and final project goals and success measures 
 
MPO’S Responsibilities: 
• 
Production and distribution of meeting announcements and advertising material  
• 
Schedule, organize, and invite attendees to the PSC meeting #1 
• 
Invite attendees to the study area tour 
• 
Schedule, organize, and invite attendees to the stakeholder interviews 
• 
Schedule and organize the evening community kick-off meeting 
• 
Publish and distribute announcements for evening Community Workshop #1 meeting 
 
TASK 3 – DATA COLLECTION, MAPPING, AND CONDITIONS/OPPORTUNITIES ASSESSMENT  
The Kimley-Horn team will conduct a multi-phased existing conditions assessment to understand 
initiatives completed to date, and evaluate the previous recommendations against anticipated future 
development. The information gathered will be used to document the East Dell Range and US 30 
corridors’ mobility system’s existing performance, that will be referenced in the development of the 
alternative mobility strategies and design solutions. 
 
Task 3.1 – Existing Document Review 
Kimley-Horn will review all existing plans, design guidelines, and standards from the City of Cheyenne 
that apply to this corridor; and compile into a single comprehensive summary. 
 
Task 3.2 – Project Mapping with Infrastructure and Utility Update 
Kimley-Horn will prepare a base map of the study area (in a format capable of being imported into the 
City’s ArcGIS system) to document the contextual analysis and upon which all engineering decisions will 

 
3 | P a g e 
 
be made. A project high-resolution aerial scaled to 1’ = 20’ will be acquired for this project. Kimley-Horn 
will rely predominantly on available existing data and resources provided by the MPO and its project 
partners. The Kimley-Horn team, with field supplemental assembled data, will also identify: 
• 
Driveway locations 
• 
Signalized intersections 
• 
Transit stops 
• 
Drainage inlets (field edit) 
• 
Stormwater system 
• 
Sanitary sewer system 
• 
Potable water system 
• 
Overhead utility systems and crossings 
• 
Other utilities 
 
Task 3.3 – Wet and Dry Utility Evaluation 
A utility assessment and upgrade analysis will be developed for the wet and dry utilities within the study 
area based on coordination plans with known utility providers (i.e., CenturyLink, Black Hills Power, 
Spectrum, and Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities) for expansion and rehabilitation of existing facilities. 
Additionally, storm water conveyance components and contributory basins of the corridor area will be 
reviewed. This utility assessment will document assumptions and known system upgrades, identify any 
significant issues, and recommend conceptual modifications that could impact future corridor 
improvements. 
 
Task 3.4 – Land Use and Context Assessment and Sociodemographic Data Update  
Kimley-Horn will assess the land use and socio-demographic changes that have occurred in the corridor 
since 2008. In this assessment, the Kimley-Horn team will update the population and employment 
assumptions from the 2008 travel demand model and use this information to update the traffic 
assumptions to test the previous 2008 recommendations.  
 
Task 3.5 – Multimodal Transportation Demand Assessment  
Kimley-Horn will evaluate the corridor, and its 2008 recommendations and mode-specific strategies.. The 
assessment will identify corridor strengths, weaknesses, opportunities, and threats analysis (SWOT). 
Elements of the assessment will include:  
 
• 
Access Management and Safety Assessment – Kimley-Horn’s safety and access management 
assessment will review the physical conditions of the corridors (driveway pacing, median 
openings, sidewalk locations, sight lines, etc.), and perform a corridor walking safety audit. The 
Kimley-Horn team will begin by reviewing three to five years of Cheyenne Police crash records, 
mapping the findings, and identifying any patterns that should be addressed within the corridor. 
In addition, the entire team will walk the corridors, and conduct visual inspections and safety 
audits. These audits enhance observations and allow the team to review a safety checklist. 
During these multidisciplinary walks the Kimley-Horn team will also foster discussions to help 
identify the best crash mitigation strategies. 
 
• 
Bicycling, Walkability, and Transit Facility (Americans with Disabilities Act [ADA])/Complete 
Street Assessment – Kimley-Horn will conduct a complete street needs assessment for both 
East Dell Range and the US 30 corridors. In this assessment, the Kimley-Horn team will evaluate 
each mode of travel’s needs and outline appropriate levels of accommodation within the 

 
4 | P a g e 
 
corridor. The stress levels of the walking and bicycle corridors will be evaluated, along with the 
accessibility of existing transit facilities on East Dell Range and US 30 associated with Cheyenne 
Transit’s Ocean Loop/ Dell Range route and the Greenway/Lincoln Way route. 
 
• 
Traffic Operations and Warrant Assessment – The Kimley-Horn team will prepare an existing 
traffic conditions and future forecast summary report. The summary report will describe the 
anticipated corridor operating conditions for the study area with the recommended 
improvements identified from the 2008 plan. Traffic volumes for the future-year traffic conditions 
will be based on the MPO information, as well as adjacent large development project traffic 
studies (such as Whitney Ranch.  
 
The future traffic analysis will determine ultimate roadway and intersection needs for the corridor and 
study intersections for the long-term planning horizon. The analysis tool to be used for this project will be 
the Synchro traffic simulation software. Existing signal system timings will be used for the current 
conditions analysis, and optimized timings will be prepared and used for the future-year analysis. Both 
sets of timings are to be approved for use by the MPO prior to conducting any evaluations. The corridor 
Synchro (SimTraffic) analysis will include the intersections listed and will involve:  
• 
anticipated travel times (delay study)  
• 
vehicle level of service (LOS)  
• 
 identifying any operational deficiencies.  
Using anticipated traffic values, Kimley-Horn will conduct signal warrant analyses for intersections to 
determine if signals are warranted in the future.  
 
INTERSECTIONS TO BE EVALUATED  
East Dell Range:  
 
 
US 30:  
College Drive   
 
 
College Drive 
Ocean Avenue  
 
 
Pershing Boulevard / Cleveland Avenue 
Ontario Avenue  
 
 
Ocean Avenue 
Van Buren Avenue  
 
 
Cady Avenue 
El Camino Drive  
 
 
Polk Avenue 
Whitney Road   
 
 
Van Buren Avenue 
US 30   
 
 
 
Hayes Avenue 
 
 
 
 
 
Alston Avenue  
Whitney Road  
Firewalker Trail  
US 30 
 
Task 3.6 – Corridor Strengths, Weaknesses, Opportunities, and Threats (SWOT) Analysis 
Kimley-Horn will provide the MPO an integrated corridor SWOT assessment of the study area for the East Dell 
Range and US 30 corridors. This assessment will provide site analysis and context maps; an issues and 
opportunities assessment; and a PowerPoint presentation summarizing the data assembled along with 
information gathered from the stakeholder interviews, first community workshop, and the Multimodal 
Demand Assessment. 
 
DELIVERABLES: 
• 
Corridor Access Management and Safety Technical Memorandum 
• 
Complete Street Needs Technical Memorandum 

 
5 | P a g e 
 
• 
Traffic Operations and Signal Warrant Technical Memorandum 
• 
Corridor SWOT Technical Memorandum and PowerPoint 
 
MPO’S RESPONSIBILITIES: 
Provide a list and programs of current and recently approved developments in the corridor 
Provide population, housing, and employment growth forecasts for Cheyenne and the metro area 
 
Task 4 – Alternative Development and Refinement 
Kimley-Horn will develop initial mode-specific strategies and potential solutions, building from the 2008 
recommendations for the East Dell Range and US 30 corridors for the community, staff, and steering 
committee’s consideration in the final set of recommendations. The goal is to integrate the multimodal 
projects, and deliver a package of strategies and actions into a single framework that aligns with 
community expectations, and is capable of being readily implementable within the City’s existing 
resources and budgeting processes. 
 
Task 4.1 – Initial Designs and Concept Alternatives 
Kimley-Horn will develop an initial set of conceptual designs for community consideration. These designs 
will include an overall corridor plan, at 50-scale, along with four conceptual roadway cross sections (20-
scale), and a conceptual intersection design at 20-scale.  
 
Task 4.2 – Community Workshop #2 
The second community design workshop will incorporate the results of: the stakeholder interviews, the 
first community workshop, and the multi-faceted technical analysis; and present initial design concepts 
of the corridors for community consideration. Feedback from this meeting will inform the Kimley-Horn 
team’s final set of recommendations. 
 
Task 4.3 – 35% Preliminary Design 
Kimley-Horn will incorporate feedback from the second community meeting and develop a refined 35% 
preliminary design of the corridor for long-term improvements. The design will address needed traffic 
operational improvements, along with long-term solutions that will ensure the corridors accommodate 
all modes of travel. This 35% design will be the basis of all cost estimates established for the project. 
 
Task 4.4 – Implementation Strategies 
The implementation plan for the East Dell Range and US 30 corridors will develop planning- level cost 
estimates, for project recommendations based on constructability. Cost estimates will be determined 
using available local and regional data sources. 
 
• 
Cost Estimates – The Kimley-Horn team will develop preliminary construction cost estimates 
for the identified improvements in the East Dell Range and US 30 corridors. 
 
• 
Prioritization and Programming – The Kimley-Horn team will categorize projects into five and 10 
horizon years to establish a clear picture of what can be implemented with City resources. The 
methodology used, project prioritization technique, and design and scheduling assumptions will 
be based on the community-supported project goals and measures of success. Projects will be 
presented so their purpose and need will be clearly communicated, providing a useful tool if 
funding situations change or new priorities are identified after this corridor plan is completed. 
 
Task 4.5 – Project Steering Committee Meeting #2 

 
6 | P a g e 
 
Kimley-Horn will facilitate the PSC’s review of the initial design concepts and technical support 
documents during an interactive workshop. 
 
DELIVERABLES: 
• 
Corridor Initial Concept Plan (50-scale) 
• 
Four initial recommended cross sections (20-scale) 
• 
Intersection concept plans (20-scale) 
• 
35% Preliminary Corridor Design Plans in CADD Format 
• 
Meeting announcement and advertising material for MPO publication and distribution 
• 
Preliminary design presentation for the Community Meeting #2 
• 
Summary notes from the Community Meeting #2 
• 
Summary notes from the PSC’s Meeting #2 
 
MPO’S RESPONSIBILITIES: 
• 
Schedule, organize, and invite attendees to Community Meeting #2 
• 
Schedule, organize, and invite attendees to the PSC’s Meeting #2 
 
Task 5 – Comprehensive Corridor Plan 
A Comprehensive Corridor Plan, with mode-specific strategies and recommended infrastructure 
improvements, will be compiled into a coherent draft summary report. The project recommendations 
and implementation strategies will then be presented to the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission for 
final review and endorsement. A final illustrative report will then be packaged and provided to MPO staff. 
 
Task 5.1 – Draft Corridor Plan and Report  
A Comprehensive Corridor Plan will be: 
1. Developed with a clear vision; 
2. Easy to access via the City’s website; 
3. Easy to read/understand; 
4. Of a reasonable page length; 
5. Subdivided into meaningful sections; 
6. Free of excess information (use appendices); and 
7. Inclusive of graphical methods for presenting content. 
 
Task 5.2 – Public Presentations and Public Hearings 
Kimley-Horn will present the draft corridor plan and report to the City and County Planning 
Commissions; the Cheyenne MPO Policy, Technical, and Citizens Advisory Committees; one City Council 
meeting; and one County Commission meeting. 
 
Task 5.3 – Final Corridor Plan and Report 
Kimley-Horn will revise the draft Corridor Plan based on comments received during the final public 
presentation and public hearings to finalize the document. 
 
DELIVERABLES: 
• 
Draft corridor plan and report in electronic PDF file format 
• 
Final report in electronic PDF file format and 10 bound hard copies 
• 
Final project presentation (PowerPoint Format) 
 

 
7 | P a g e 
 
MPO’S RESPONSIBILITIES: 
• 
Distribute the draft East Dell Range and US 30 Corridor Plan and Report to partner review 
agencies 
• 
Facilitate timely review from each review partner 
• 
Provide one consolidated set of review comments to the draft East Dell Range and US 30 Corridor 
Plan 
• 
Schedule and provide staff report for the Cheyenne Planning Commission summarizing the East 
Dell Range and US 30 Corridor Master Plan

for Planning and Engineering Services in Support of the East Dell Range and US 30 Corridor Study

as follows:

The City owns land and buildings near Frontier Park, which have been used for parking,
camping and other activities by CFD. Specifically, the City owns:
a.

Road on the south, Interstate 25 on the west, the Governor’s Mansion property on the
northwest corner, and the Airport Golf Course on the north, northeast and east.

The “E” lot parking area described as north of Lions Park Drive, south of the
Municipal Swimming Pool and east of Carey Avenue.

The City ofCheyenne Kiwanis Community House lot located in Lions Park, between
the Putt Hurt miniature golf course and Sloan’s Lake.
d.
Paved parking lots located adjacent to the Boy Scouts’ meeting house, between
Sloan’s Lake and the Western Walkway, which for the purposes of this agreement
are named “Tow” lots.
2.

parcels and structures. This MOU is substantially similar to earlier agreements.

a.

Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration through the Monday after the celebration ends.

lot.

facilitate access to the Cheyenne Frontier Days Old West Museum and relieve
parking pressure in “the Avenues.”

responsibility of CR).

injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse
effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to
the extent caused by (1) CFD’s breach of any term or provision of this MOU, or (2) any
negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by CFD, or its employees or subcontractors in
the performance of this MOU. CFD acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation
to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph.
11.
This MOU contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or
conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.
12.
The parties agree that this MOU shall not become effective and enforceable until it has been
approved by the parties’ respective governing bodies.

names by and through their respective authorized representatives.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_29532995.pdf
Approved as to
ATTACHMENT A
/
CONTRACT NO.
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE.
LARAMIE COUNTY SCHOOL DISTRICT NUMBER ONE. AND
CHEYENNE FRONTIER DAYS. INC.
1.
Parties. This Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”) is made and entered into by and
between the City of Cheyenne, a municipality organized and existing under the laws of the State of
Wyoming (“City”), whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, Laramie
County School District Number One (“District”), whose address is 2810 House Avenue, Cheyenne,
Wyoming 82001, and Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc., a Wyoming corporation (“CFD”), whose
address is P.O. Box 2477, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82003.
2.
Purpose. The purpose ofthis MOU is to provide a means for the City to provide enhanced
public transportation services during the 2018 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration by leasing school
buses from the District for use by the District’s drivers under contract with CFD.
3.
Term of MOU. This MOU shall, when approved and executed by the parties hereto, remain
in hill force and effect through July 30, 2018. The payment and indemnification provisions of this
MOU shall survive the termination hereof.
4.
Payment. Upon receipt of an itemized invoice from the District, the City agrees to pay the
District for the costs of operation of the buses for the duration of this MOU. Such costs shall
include, but not necessarily be limited to, ftel, depreciation and maintenance. In no event will the
total paid by the City to the District under this MOU exceed the total cost of fuel, depreciation and
maintenance incurred by the District in leasing buses pursuant to this MOU. CFD shall reimburse
the City for these expenses in accordance with a separate agreement between the City and CFD.
5.
Responsibilities of District.
A.
The District shall furnish the following:
i.
Twenty-five (25) buses, each with a minimum capacity of seventy-two (72)
passengers, three (3) wheelchair accessible buses, three (3) suburbans, and one (1) trailer,
from July 19, 2018 through July 30, 2018, in support of CFD ‘Park and Ride” program.
From these buses, a specific type and number of buses will also be used for the following
activities:
a.
Four (4) buses, each with a minimum capacity of seventy-two (72)
passengers, for the “Cowboy Triangle” from July 19, 2018 through July 30, 2018;
and
b.
Four (4) buses, each with a minimum capacity of seventy-two (72)
passengers, and one (1) wheelchair accessible bus, for the Governor’s Art Auction
on Thursday, July 19, 2018; and
1

c.
Two (2) wheelchair accessible buses for the Fort D.A. Russell Days
from July 20, 2018 through July 22, 2018; and
d.
Seven (7) buses, each with a minimum capacity of seventy-two (72)
passengers, for the Thunderbird Airshow on Wednesday, July 25, 2018.
ii.
Four (4) buses, Type A (shorter buses) from July 19, 2018 through July 30,
2018, for the Volunteer Shuttle Operation from Central High School to Frontier Park.
B.
The number of buses listed above represent the maximum number of buses to be in
operation at any one time.
6.
Qualifications ofDriven. For the term ofthis MOU, the District shall ensure that the buses
leased under this MOU are driven by qualified District personnel who possess valid commercial
driver’s licenses for the type of vehicle operated, and who will act as temporary employees of CFD
during the term of and for all purposes associated with this MOU.
7.
Special Provisions.
A.
CFD will provide property, casualty and liability insurance coverage for the buses
to be leased by the District to the City and CFD and for all drivers designated by the District to
operate said buses for the purposes stated in this MOU, and to name the City and the District as
additional insureds and loss payees on all property, casualty and liability insurance policies to be
provided by CFD pursuant to a separate contract with the City.
B.
It is expressly agreed and understood that insurance coverages provided by CFD
pursuant to this Section shall be primary and not contributory.
C.
It is expressly agreed and understood that CFD and the City will fully release,
indemnif’ and hold harmless the District and its officers, agents, employees, successors and assigns
from any and all causes of action, claims, liabilities, demands or expenses arising out of the use of
the District school buses under this MOU.
8.
General Provisions.
A.
Amendments.
Any party may request changes in this MOU. Any changes,
modifications, revisions or amendments to this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by and
between parties to this MOU shall be incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by
all parties to this MOU.
B.
Avplicable Law. The construction, interpretation and enforcement ofthis MOU shall
be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have
jurisdiction over any action arising out this MOU and over the parties, and the venue shall be the
First Judicial District Court, Laramie County, Wyoming.
2

C.
Assignments. This MOU shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit ofthe parties
hereto and their respective heirs, successors, transferees, and permitted assignees. No party shall
have the right to assign any rights or obligations under this MOU without the express written
consent of all of the other parties.
D.
Availability of Funds.
The payment obligations of the City and the District are
conditioned upon the availability of government finds, which are appropriated or allocated for the
payment of this obligation.
If finds are not allocated and available for the continuance of the
services performed by either party, the City or the District may terminate the MOU at the end of the
period for which the fUnds are available. The City and the District shall notify’ the other parties to
this MOU at the earliest possible time of the services which will or may be affected by a shortage
of fimds. No penalty shall accrue to the City or the District in the event this provision is exercised,
and neither the District nor the City shall be subjected to liability for any damages, costs, or
expenses claimed to have been incurred as a result oftermination under this Section.
E.
Entirety ofAgreement. This MOU, consisting offour (4) pages, represents the entire
and integrated agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations
and agreements, whether written or oral.
F.
Force Maieure. No party shall be liable to perform under the MOU if such failure
arises out of causes beyond control and without the fault or negligence of said party. Such causes
may include, but are not limited to, Acts of God or the public enemy, fires, floods, epidemics,
quarantine restrictions, freight embargoes, and unusually severe weather.
G.
Notices. All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU shall be in
writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person at the addresses indicated
above.
H.
Sovereign/Governmental Immunity.
The City of Cheyenne and Laramie County
School District Number One, do not waive governmental immunity by entering into this MOU, and
each filly retains all immunities and defenses provided by law with respect to any action based on
or occurring as a result of this MOU.
I.
Third Pam’ Beneficiary Rights.
The parties do not intend to create in any other
individual or entity the status of third party beneficiary, and this MOU shall not be construed so as
to create such status. The rights, duties and obligations contained in this MOU shall operate only
between the parties to this MOU, and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this MOU.
The provisions of this MOU are intended only to assist the parties in determining and performing
their obligations under this MOU. The parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only
parties signatory to this MOU shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU,
to seek any remedy arising out of a party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition
of this MOU. or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU.
3

9.
Signatures.
In witness whereof, the parties to this MOU through their duly authorized
representatives have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below, and certify that they
have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of this MOU as set forth herein.
The effective date of this MOU shall be the date of the last signature affixed to this page.
CITY OF CHEYENNE
Date
Marian J. On, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
Carol Intlekofer. City Clerk
LAR&MIE COUNTY SCHOOL
DISTRICT NUMBER ONE
Date
Chairman, Board of Trustees
CHEYENNE FRONTIER DAYS, NC.
Date
By its CEO
